# Session Log

One entry per session. Append-only. Old entries archive at phase audits, never delete.

Format:
```
## Session N (Package P)
- Produced: [what shipped]
- Decisions: [D-numbers from decisions.md, or "none"]
- Tests: [pass/fail summary]
- Reflection: [one or two sentences. What was learned, what should change.]
```

## S48b — Intent-classification evaluation substrate
roles: analyst, PM, architect, engineer, technical writer
mode: build session, executing briefs/p13/s48b.md

S48b closed Thread 2 of the parallel post-S47 forward path with the intent-classification evaluation substrate at `contexts/intent_classification_evaluation/` per D137. The substrate answers component-quality questions ("does model X classify Padhanam's intent surface reliably") in minutes against a fixed reference set rather than through multi-minute integration smokes. The S46 (qwen2.5:7b intent-extraction blind-spot) and S47 (qwen2.5:14b hardware-viability) findings were both surfaced by integration smokes asked to do work the architecture is structurally designed to split; S48b closes that methodology gap by building the dedicated evaluation surface the port-and-adapter shape already supported. Six commits land Option B (slim load-bearing version): charter (D137 plus architecture plus three captures methodology entries plus brief preservation); shared_kernel intent-classification primitive extraction plus single-source-of-truth structural test; substrate code (domain plus application plus ports plus Postgres plus Alembic 0022); CLI sub-app plus YAML gold-set fixture plus loader; tenant-isolation harness plus new layers-intent-class-eval import-linter contract; live-stack exercise verifying the substrate against tenant_a with two models. The full P11 mirror's revision-lifecycle gold sets and cross-run-comparison CLI subcommand defer to Phase 2-B+ at named activation triggers per D137 alternatives (c) and (d).

- Produced: Six commits. (1) `docs(charter): land P13 S48b intent-classification evaluation substrate (D137)` (b2e4970) covering D137 at decisions.md, the architecture.md sub-section, three captures methodology candidate entries (component-quality vs integration smoke promotion-ready at two-instance evidence; scope-discipline-at-brief-authoring first-instance; structural-test single-source-of-truth second-instance), the placeholder S48b in-flight marker, and the brief preservation at briefs/p13/s48b.md. (2) `feat(p13/s48b): extract intent-classification prompt and schema to shared_kernel` (91511dd) moving INTENT_EXTRACTION_SCHEMA + EXTRACTION_PREAMBLE + build_extraction_prompt to `shared_kernel/intent_classification.py`, refactoring the cell to consume the shared primitive, plus a four-scenario contract harness at `tests/contract/intent_classification/test_single_source_of_truth.py` asserting Python-identity (`is`) across consumers. (3) `feat(p13/s48b): intent-classification evaluation substrate (domain, ports, app, Postgres, Alembic 0022)` (cfd3450) landing 24 new files covering domain (gold_set, evaluation_run, evaluation_result, metrics), application (3 use cases plus audit_events), ports (3 Protocols), Postgres adapters (repository + reader + _tables) with bound-tenant defence-in-depth, Alembic migration 0022_intent_class_eval_substrate creating three per-tenant tables, and 20 domain-layer unit tests. (4) `feat(p13/s48b): CLI sub-app, YAML gold-set fixture, and fixture loader` (7aa8d8f) landing the typer sub-app at `apps/cli/_intent_classification_eval.py` with `eval start/get/list` subcommands registered under `padhanam intent-classification-eval`, plus the 40-entry gold-set fixture at `tests/fixtures/intent_classification/gold_set.yaml` (12 CreateCase, 12 AddDataPoint including the 4 S46 blind-spot phrasings, 8 ReviseDataPoint, 8 Unclear), plus the YamlGoldSetReader adapter. (5) `test(p13/s48b): tenant_isolation harness + layers-intent-class-eval import-linter contract` (8749442) landing six isolation scenarios (3 structural across per-tenant DBs plus control-plane, 3 behavioural across bound-tenant defence-in-depth ValueError surfaces) and four import-linter contract additions (new layers-intent-class-eval hexagonal; intent_classification_evaluation added to three cross-context independence contracts plus the domain-purity contract). (6) `docs(p13/s48b): live-stack intent-classification evaluation against tenant_a` (this commit) landing the smoke document at `docs/smoke/p13_s48b_intent_classification_eval.md`, this session log entry, and the S48b close marker.

- Live-stack exercise outcome — three evaluation runs against tenant_a. (a) `gpt-4o-mini` ran to completion in ~75 seconds across all 40 entries: 39/40 correct (97.50% overall accuracy); per-class create_case 100%, add_data_point 100% (including all 4 S46 blind-spot phrasings classified correctly), revise_data_point 87.5% (1 miss; debatable gold-set entry), unclear 100%; latency profile min 970ms / avg 1.89s / p50 1.63s / p95 3.43s / max 6.14s; zero parse failures; total spend <$0.001 against the OpenAI account at published rates. (b) `qwen2.5:7b` failed twice at first-call cold-start timeout (91.5s and 92.2s vs the 30s REAL_TIME_REQUIRED tier budget); the substrate's runner-level failure-capture absorbed both timeouts, marked the runs failed with structured failure_reason, emitted the run.fail audit event. The qwen2.5:7b operational verdict is "not viable at REAL_TIME_REQUIRED tier on this hardware" — the production cell uses the same tier budget, so operator-facing cold-start cascades would face identical timeouts. (c) Audit chain extended with 6 events (one start + one terminal-transition per run); chain integrity holds at 245 events / 245 distinct hashes / single genesis from 2026-05-12.

- Decisions: D137 (intent-classification evaluation substrate at contexts/intent_classification_evaluation/) plus the architecture.md addition plus captures methodology candidates. No D-entries beyond D137 at S48b.

- Tests: 1776 unit + contract tests pass (+22 from S48a close baseline of 1754: +4 single-source-of-truth contract harness, +12 domain-layer unit tests on the substrate, +6 tenant-isolation contract scenarios). 33 of 33 import-linter contracts kept (+1 layers-intent-class-eval). AST enforcement green. Alembic migration 0022 applied successfully to both tenant data planes.

- Reflection (substrate placement verification, prompt 1): The recommended cut (a) at pre-write reconciliation Surface 1 — new bounded context at `contexts/intent_classification_evaluation/` per D127 alternative (d) — held without revision. Intent-classification evaluation and retrieval evaluation are distinct architectural concerns (different evidence source; different metric shapes; different gold-set authoring); a single evaluation umbrella context would have absorbed too much for one instance per the build-at-second-instance discipline. The placement decision was clean and did not require a D-entry alternative-considered update beyond what D137 already records.

- Reflection (gold-set authoring discipline, prompt 2): The 40 entries authored cleanly across the four intent classes with the four S46 blind-spot phrasings landing as explicit AddDataPointIntent entries at index 12-15. The S40b methodology entry ("metric-threshold expectations need structural-understanding grounding") fired weakly at S48b — the single revise_data_point miss at entry 29 ("The hiring search status changed: offer extended yesterday" classified as add_data_point) surfaces a gold-set entry whose expected-class is debatable. The phrase "status changed: offer extended yesterday" could legitimately be read as adding a new status note rather than revising an existing one; the gold-set author chose revise_data_point because "changed" + "yesterday" implies update, but the model's add_data_point reading is also coherent. Two structural improvements possible at future work: (a) tighten gold-set entries at the operator-authoring layer to remove expected-class ambiguity; (b) extend the schema to ask the model for a disambiguation discriminator between add vs revise on borderline phrasings. Neither is in scope at S48b. The discipline observation: even a 40-entry gold set surfaces at least one ambiguity at the operator-authoring layer; future per-tenant gold sets (D137 alternative (c) trigger) should expect the same and provision authoring-time disambiguation tools.

- Reflection (per-model evaluation outcome, prompt 3): **Substantive paragraph because the result is load-bearing.** The two-model comparison directly validates the S48a swap decision component-side. gpt-4o-mini classified the four S46 blind-spot phrasings correctly (4/4 add_data_point) at warm-call latency 1.5-2.3 seconds, dropped one borderline revise_data_point entry (debatable gold-set entry), and ran the full 40-entry evaluation in ~75 seconds for <$0.001 against the OpenAI account. qwen2.5:7b could not complete a single classification within the 30-second REAL_TIME_REQUIRED tier budget on this hardware (both runs failed at 91-92 second cold-start timeouts). The component-quality verdict for the current Phase 2-A operator-dogfooding model choice is unambiguous: gpt-4o-mini is operationally fit; qwen2.5:7b is not. The substrate's load-bearing claim ("answer model-choice questions in minutes") delivered in 75 seconds for a positive verdict and in 92 seconds for a clean failure verdict. Both outcomes are procurement-grade evidence the audit chain captures.

- Reflection (confidence-value calibration evidence, prompt 4): gpt-4o-mini populated the `confidence` field consistently across all 40 entries (no nulls); confidence values clustered in the 0.7-0.95 band for correct classifications. The single revise_data_point miss had confidence 0.85 — the model was confident in the wrong classification, which is exactly the failure mode confidence-aware composition is most exposed to (Case 1 high-confidence-but-wrong). The architectural defense at D134 remains the right cut: pattern 2 (suggestion-as-question) handles medium-confidence wrong classifications; only the high-confidence wrong cases (~2.5% of the gold set here) bypass that defense. Forward calibration substrate evidence: a per-model confidence-vs-correctness scatter plot at substrate altitude would surface model-specific calibration patterns; the substrate's data is now in place at `intent_class_evaluation_results` to compute that. Brief observation; not in scope at S48b.

- Reflection (methodology surfaces, prompt 5): **Substantive paragraph because three methodology candidates land.** First, the **scope-discipline-at-brief-authoring** observation from the operator's framing-time methodology line: the S48b brief committed the full P11-mirror as default scope; Surface 1 reconciliation treated the mirror as precedent rather than testing whether each piece served a current need; the scope question surfaced only when the full file count emerged at the brief-versus-codebase reading. Captured at log/captures.md as first-instance, adjacent to but distinct from interface-versus-implementation. Second, the **structural-test single-source-of-truth binding** pattern fired its second instance at S48b commit 2 (Python-identity assertion across consumer imports for the shared intent-classification primitive). The first instance was the no-numeric-threshold-literals AST test at S47 addendum commit 4f74509; the second-instance promotion threshold is met. Third, the **component-quality-vs-integration-smoke** discipline has now landed its first concrete implementation (D137 at S48b) — two-instance evidence from S46 and S47 plus the structural surface from D137. All three forward to the post-S48 hygiene methodology review.

- Reflection (path-naming discipline operational evidence, prompt 6): Surface 2 caught no path-naming drift at S48b framing or commit-drafting time. The brief's named paths (`contexts/intent_classification_evaluation/`, `apps/cli/_intent_classification_eval.py`, `tests/fixtures/intent_classification/`, `tests/contract/tenant_isolation/test_intent_class_eval_isolation.py`) all matched the actual conventions verified against the existing eight contexts and the existing apps/cli/_* file convention. Brief observation: the path-naming discipline is now mechanical enough that drift is rare at framing-time when the operator's brief has been authored against the actual repo state. Recurrence test continues.

methodology: Three captures-promotion-ready candidates land at S48b — the component-quality-vs-integration-smoke pattern now has two-instance evidence plus its first structural implementation in code (D137); the structural-test-binding pattern lands its second instance (symbol-identity at S48b commit 2 after the no-numeric-thresholds AST test at S47 addendum); the scope-discipline-at-brief-authoring pattern lands as first-instance observation captured during framing. All three forward to the post-S48 hygiene methodology review; the component-quality-vs-integration-smoke discipline is promotion-ready for `charter/methodology.md` per the existing two-instance-promotion convention.

## S48a — REAL_TIME_REQUIRED swap to gpt-4o-mini and smoke revalidation
roles: engineer, technical writer
mode: build session, executing briefs/p13/s48a.md

S48a — Thread 1 of the parallel post-S47 forward path — swapped the REAL_TIME_REQUIRED tier model pin from `qwen2.5:14b` to the hosted `gpt-4o-mini` per D133's gateway-as-resolution-point shape, then re-executed the S47 multi-turn cell smoke against tenant_a. The S47 smoke had surfaced `qwen2.5:14b`'s commodity-hardware viability gap (progressive 28s→361s slowdown); S48a verifies whether the convergence's Response A disposition closes operationally at a hosted model rather than a local one. **Verdict: operational dogfooding loop unblocked at `gpt-4o-mini`.** 4/4 AddDataPointIntent phrasings classified correctly at 1.5-3 s warm latency; convergence concern 5 closes operationally.

- Produced: One feat commit (`feat(p13/s48a): swap REAL_TIME_REQUIRED tier pin to gpt-4o-mini`, d620692) covering three configuration surfaces: `padhanam/config/inference.py` line 70 pin swap (qwen2.5:14b → gpt-4o-mini); `ops/litellm/config.yaml` model_list gains gpt-4o-mini routing entry referencing `os.environ/OPENAI_API_KEY`; `compose.yaml` litellm service gains `OPENAI_API_KEY: ${OPENAI_API_KEY:-}` env passthrough. Plus `.env.example` OPENAI_API_KEY block surfacing the new credential to operators, the model_registry docstring refresh acknowledging qwen2.5:14b's local-development-fallback role, and three test updates (one rename + assertion swap, two comment refreshes). The brief's intended chore commit shape upgraded to feat because the brief's surface-3 reconciliation found that the one-line pin change wouldn't work operationally without the parallel gateway and compose changes (the exact drift class the S47 smoke captured at `log/captures.md`). Plus one `docs(p13/s48a)` commit (this commit) landing the executed-evidence smoke document append at `docs/smoke/p13_s47_multi_turn_cell_end_to_end.md` under a new "## S48a re-execution at `gpt-4o-mini`" section, the qwen2.5:14b captures-entry resolution-line update (resolved via swap to gpt-4o-mini), three new captures entries (resolver disambiguation rendering on duplicate-title collision; cancel-then-fresh-turn double-cost behaviour; auto-expire-prior-pending positive substrate observation), this session-log entry, and the current-package S48a close marker.

- Smoke outcome — all stages verified end-to-end. Stage 1 environment: ngrok, Twilio Sandbox, gateway-lists-gpt-4o-mini, end-to-end probe through gateway → OpenAI in 2.15 s. Stage 2 CreateCaseIntent regression: cell classified at medium confidence, rendered Case 2 shape-aware clarification, cell-call latency 2.19 s. Stage 4 (first instance) confirmation resolution: pending RESOLVED in 16 ms (deterministic confirm path), case `acb449a0` created, cited confirmation reply rendered (`Recorded a new case: Q3 portfolio review. — ref acb449a0 · intake ea1563fc · 10:06 UTC`). **Stage 3 four-phrasing battery: 4/4 classifications correct** with `intent_type=add_data_point`, `data_point_type=GOAL`, `value_text="ship Wave 1 by end of May"`, and case_reference variants preserving the user's literal phrasing (`Q3 review` / `Q3 portfolio review` / `Q3 portfolio review case` / `Q3 portfolio review`); warm LLM call latency 1.5-2.3 s, cold 2.9-4.1 s. Stage 4 (second instance) surfaced the duplicate-title resolver-disambiguation finding (three cases titled `Q3 portfolio review` from S46/S47/S48a; the resolver's rendering uses only `title` and produced three identical disambiguation strings; no data_point was written). Stage 5 cancellation surfaced the cancel-then-fresh-turn double-cost behaviour (deterministic resolve in 14 ms + fresh-turn LLM in 3.77 s on the bare `no`). Stage 6 audit chain integrity: 239 events / 239 distinct hashes / single genesis from 2026-05-12; chain holds. Final tenant_a state: intakes 29→40 (+11), cases 7→8 (+1, `acb449a0`), data_points 3→3 (zero, resolver bailed), messages 36→56 (+20), pending_clarifications 2→7 (+5), tenant_audit 196→239 (+43).

- Decisions: No new D-entries. The session executes against D133's existing commitment (gateway-as-resolution-point with static configuration at Phase 2-A) and the model registry's pre-seeded `gpt-4o-mini` entry from S47 commit 4db4476.

- Tests: 1750 unit + contract passing post-swap (matches S47 close baseline exactly; the swap is configuration-only, no new test assertions added beyond the one renamed test asserting the new pin); 32 of 32 import-linter contracts kept; AST enforcement green.

- Reflection (classification reliability at `gpt-4o-mini` against the S46 blind-spot phrasings, prompt 1): **substantive paragraph because the result is load-bearing.** `gpt-4o-mini` classified `add_data_point` correctly across all four structural variants: template (`Add a goal to the Q3 review: …`), verb-first-with-full-title (`Add this goal to the Q3 portfolio review: …`), target-front-loaded (`For the Q3 portfolio review case, add a goal: …`), and the structurally-hardest no-verb-noun-phrase (`Ship Wave 1 by end of May — goal for the Q3 portfolio review.`). Each cascade populated all four intent slots correctly. S46's `qwen2.5:7b` was 0/2 on the template phrasing; S47's `qwen2.5:14b` couldn't validate because of latency timeouts. The intent-extraction reliability finding from S46/S47 closes at S48a via the configuration-only path the convergence's Response A described, with the hosted-inference variant resolving the hardware-viability gap. Phase 2-A operator-dogfooding's AddDataPointIntent surface is operationally usable.

- Reflection (latency profile against operator dogfooding viability, prompt 2): warm cell-call latency at `gpt-4o-mini` landed in the 1.5-2.3 s band; cold (first-call-after-restart) landed in the 2.9-4.1 s band. End-to-end inbound→outbound including DB writes and WhatsApp delivery lands at 1.7-4.1 s. Confirm-path orchestration (no LLM call) lands at 271-294 ms. Compared to S46's `qwen2.5:7b` warm at 8-9 s / cold at 22.95 s and S47's `qwen2.5:14b` at 28 s → 361 s progressive, `gpt-4o-mini` is comfortably in the operator-dogfooding-viable band. The dogfooding loop is unblocked operationally.

- Reflection (confidence-aware composition under the new model, prompt 3): the cell's self-reported confidence field is populated consistently across all classifications, landing in the medium band (0.5 ≤ c < 0.8) for both CreateCaseIntent and all four AddDataPointIntent phrasings. This is the same band the S47 smoke observed at `qwen2.5:14b` on the same phrasing classes, which suggests the medium-band landing is a calibration property of the cell's prompt-and-schema design rather than the model's capability. The cell's Case 2 (shape-aware clarification) renders correctly throughout; D134's three-case discipline operates as intended. The ConfidenceCalculator port's swap-readiness holds: a future adapter (e.g. one that calibrates against a held-out set rather than reading self-reported confidence) could swap in via the port's adapter slot without cell-side change. No captures entry added for the calibration observation — single instance, not promotion-threshold.

- Reflection (cost profile at operator-dogfooding volume, prompt 4): the smoke session consumed approximately 10 LLM cascades (Stage 2 + 4 fresh-turn classifications at Stage 3 + 1 cancel-fresh-turn at Stage 5 + a few corrections); each cascade carries ~$0.0001 input + output cost at gpt-4o-mini's published $0.150 input / $0.600 output per 1M tokens. Total smoke spend: well under $0.01. Operator dogfooding at one user's volume (estimated 50-200 cascades/day) projects to <$1/day, well within the static-configuration commitment's budget bracket. The model_registry's cost-per-call metadata is honest at the gpt-4o-mini row (verified at smoke close; no drift). The brief's reflection prompt 4 verdict: cost profile validates the architectural expectation.

- Reflection (methodology surfaces, prompt 5): substantive paragraph because three observations land. **First**, the swap that the brief framed as "one configuration line, chore commit shape" required three configuration surfaces (inference.py pin + gateway model_list entry + compose env passthrough) plus an `.env.example` addition for operator-facing documentation. The brief's pre-write reconciliation surface 3 (LiteLLM API for gpt-4o-mini) correctly anticipated this — the reconciliation guidance "if more is needed at the configuration surface, the swap is not a pure chore and the commit shape upgrades to feat" fired exactly as designed. The interface-versus-implementation discipline (now at 6+ instances of evidence) caught the discrepancy at framing rather than commit-draft time; the brief was honestly framed and the upgrade was clean. **Second**, the smoke-document append-not-replace protocol (brief's surface 5) was the first application of the pattern at a model-choice surface; the append cleanly preserved S47's executed evidence as historical record while the S48a section captured the new evidence. **Pattern recurrence note** at first instance only — a future model-choice surface change (S48c or beyond) is the test. **Third**, the smoke surfaced three substrate findings (resolver disambiguation rendering; cancel-then-fresh-turn double-cost; auto-expire positive observation) that are structurally separable from the model-choice question. Smoke discipline: per-stage executed evidence keeps substrate findings cleanly distinct from the model-swap verdict, which is exactly what allows the verdict to be honest ("operational unblock at `gpt-4o-mini`") without conflating the resolver finding into the verdict. No methodology promotion at S48a; observation forwarded to phase-2-audit-inputs at session close per the existing discipline.

methodology: The brief's pre-write reconciliation surface 3 firing correctly at draft time — the LiteLLM gateway routing question caught the "swap is more than one line" reality before any commit landed — is the seventh interface-versus-implementation instance the run has now recorded, and the first at a model-choice configuration surface (the pattern's prior surfaces were code path, schema location, adapter shape). The interface-versus-implementation discipline now has demonstrated coverage across charter altitude (the convergence conversation), code altitude (S47 framing), and configuration altitude (S48a framing). Phase 2-A close hygiene's methodology-promotion path inherits a candidate with broad surface coverage.

## S47 smoke — UX convergence substrate live-stack verification
roles: engineer, technical writer
mode: strategic-mode-continuation (operational execution evidence continuing the S47 + addendum close)

The S47 substrate (D133 dispatch port + gateway-as-resolution-point + model registry; D134 confidence-aware response composition + ConfidenceCalculator + PendingClarification; D135 domain-content channel-format rendering; D136 multi-channel UX primitives; S47 addendum's ThresholdResolver port) verified end-to-end against tenant_a on a freshly-rebuilt `padhanam-api` image (digest `sha256:be1e2c09…`, advanced from the S47-base `sha256:ce2928c2…` for three smoke-driven substrate fixes). Stages 1+2 landed the full multi-turn cascade live; Stages 3-8 closed-with-findings under separately-captured operational constraints. Three substrate gaps surfaced and committed at d63f8a5; two operational findings captured at `log/captures.md` rather than fixed in-session.

- Produced: One smoke-driven substrate-fixes commit (`feat(p13/s47): smoke-driven substrate fixes`, d63f8a5) covering the cell `originating_intake_id` threading from webhook through to the PendingClarification insert (closing the FK-violation crash the brief comment had anticipated but not resolved against the migration's `fk_pending_clar_intake_id` constraint); the LiteLLM gateway `model_list` seed for qwen2.5:14b at `ops/litellm/config.yaml` (closing the model-registry-vs-gateway-routing drift the smoke surfaced on the first cell invocation); the `REAL_TIME_REQUIRED_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` env passthrough on the `padhanam-api` compose block plus the new image digest pin. Plus one `docs(p13/s47-smoke)` commit (this commit) landing the executed-evidence smoke document at `docs/smoke/p13_s47_multi_turn_cell_end_to_end.md`, two new captures entries at `log/captures.md` (qwen2.5:14b operator-dogfooding viability on commodity hardware; model-registry-vs-LiteLLM-gateway-routing drift class methodology candidate), this session-log entry, and the current-package S47 smoke close annotation.

- Smoke outcome — Stages 1+2 verified end-to-end. Multi-turn cascade with PendingClarification persistence + lifecycle confirmation: inbound `Start a case for the Q3 portfolio review.` (00:14:09 UTC) → cell classified at medium confidence → shape-aware clarification rendered `I think you want to start a case for 'Q3 portfolio review'. Is that right? (yes / no)` (00:14:58 UTC) → PendingClarification `c612afe5-…` persisted with `originating_intake_id = 1c1203cc-…` (real intake row, FK satisfied) → operator's `yes` reply (00:16:51 UTC) → cell consulted PendingClarificationReader at turn-open, found active PENDING, classified reply as confirm, resolved pending, executed create_case orchestration → cited confirmation `Recorded a new case: Q3 portfolio review. — ref 5d4c3092 · intake 874eb027 · 00:16 UTC` (00:16:53 UTC). Case `5d4c3092-…` created; pending transitioned PENDING→RESOLVED with `resolved_at` populated; two PendingClarification lifecycle audit events emitted (`messaging.pending_clarification.create` + `…resolve`). Stage 3 (cancellation) closed-with-findings: attempt 3a hit Case 3 cleanly (de-facto Stage 4 evidence); attempt 3b's cell call latency exceeded the bumped 120s tier timeout (361s LiteLLM-observed; dispatch port's failure-capture worked correctly at the boundary). Stage 4 (low-confidence/parse-failure) de-facto verified by attempt 3a's clean Case-3 rendering. Stage 5 webhook contract verified at every recorded inbound (2xx in 27-282ms regardless of cell-run duration). Stage 7 audit-chain integrity verified at smoke close (192 events, 192 distinct hashes, zero duplicates). Final tenant_a state: intakes 17→28 (+11), cases 6→7 (+1), messages 21→34 (+13), pending_clarifications 0→1, tenant_audit 165→192 (+27).

- Decisions: No new D-entries. The smoke produces operational evidence for existing commitments (D133, D134, D135, D136, S47 addendum's ThresholdResolver) plus two captures entries forwarded for post-S47 hygiene or next strategic-mode session disposition.

- Tests: 1750 unit + contract passing post-fixes (+175 from S47 close baseline of 1575, reflecting the addendum's ThresholdResolver tests plus the smoke-driven fixes' assertions); 32 of 32 import-linter contracts kept (no new contracts); AST enforcement green.

- Reflection (substrate verification, prompt 1): Stages 1+2 validate D133's dispatch port, D134's three-case discipline + PendingClarification lifecycle, D135's rendering pattern at the WhatsApp channel adapter, and the S47 addendum's ThresholdResolver port consumption — all at the live stack against tenant_a. The combined multi-turn cascade (inbound → clarification → confirmation → cited orchestration reply) lands as a single coherent flow with audit-chain integrity intact across all six lifecycle events. The load-bearing D134 commitment — pattern-2 binding at the intent-classification gate via confidence-aware composition — operationally activates: the cell renders `Is that right? (yes / no)` rather than acting confidently on an uncertain classification, exactly the failure mode the convergence's Response C addresses. The substrate works.

- Reflection (FK-violation finding, prompt 2): The smoke's first significant finding surfaced at the first cell Case 2 invocation. The S47 commit-5 cell's `_dispatch_clarify_with_pending` minted a fresh UUID for `PendingClarification.originating_intake_id`; the migration's `fk_pending_clar_intake_id` FK constraint rejected the insert with `ForeignKeyViolationError`. The brief comment had anticipated the limitation ("Phase 2-A compromise: mint a fresh UUID as the pending's originating_intake_id slot when no upstream intake id has been threaded through") but had not resolved it against the migration's FK constraint. The dispatch-port's failure-capture worked correctly at the boundary — the exception logged structurally with the caller's context dict, the webhook stayed 2xx, the FK violation surfaced cleanly in the api logs. Fix landed at d63f8a5: the webhook handler now passes the inbound `intake_id` to the cell's constructor; the cell uses it as the pending's `originating_intake_id`, satisfying the FK and giving the pending a structurally-honest audit anchor back to the inbound message. This is the kind of structural gap a unit-test cannot catch — the unit test for the cell's Case 2 path can construct a fake repository and accept any UUID, while the migration's FK constraint operates at the persistence layer outside the unit-test boundary. Contract harness extension (a per-tenant integration test verifying PendingClarification lifecycle against the real `pending_clarifications` table) would close this gap class; deferred to post-S47 hygiene.

- Reflection (model-registry-vs-gateway-routing drift, prompt 3): The second significant finding was the model-registry seed at `padhanam/config/model_registry.py` (per D133's audit-and-future-policy substrate) not propagating to the LiteLLM gateway's `model_list` at `ops/litellm/config.yaml`. The smoke caught the drift on the first cell call after the S47-base image was rebuilt — `litellm.BadRequestError: Invalid model name passed in model=qwen2.5:14b`. The fix at d63f8a5 added the gateway routing entry, but the underlying drift class remains structurally unprotected: nothing enforces that model_registry entries also appear in the gateway's model_list. Captured at `log/captures.md` as a methodology candidate; the proposed structural test (parse both files, assert set intersection) lives at `tests/contract/inference/test_model_registry_gateway_sync.py` per the captures entry's forward-fix candidate.

- Reflection (qwen2.5:14b hardware-viability finding, prompt 4): The third significant finding has S47-Response-A implications. The model bump per D133 commit 6 to qwen2.5:14b is operationally non-viable on this hardware: progressive call latency 28s → 48s → 67s → 361s suggests memory pressure or cache thrashing at the 9.7 GB resident model against the 16 GB total RAM. Phase 2-A operator dogfooding requires 10-30 second responsiveness at the WhatsApp surface; 6-minute latencies break the dogfooding loop. The S47 substrate is unaffected — even at the 14b pin, the cell at medium-confidence classification renders shape-aware clarification rather than acting confidently (D134 Response C operating correctly regardless of which model the gateway routes to). What does not validate is **Response A's static-configuration disposition** on this machine. Captured at `log/captures.md` with three forward paths (hosted inference; per-model tier-timeout overrides; constrained classification surface). The captures entry stays as forward observation rather than triggering a charter change; the existing convergence captures-entry from 2026-05-22 [S46 smoke] names all three responses and is not superseded.

- Reflection (Stage 3 cancellation closure-with-findings, prompt 5): The intended Stage 3 cancellation path was not exercised live because both attempts hit findings rather than the cancellation flow itself. Attempt 3a (`Maybe…`) routed to Case 3 cleanly — useful de-facto Stage 4 evidence but not the cancellation. Attempt 3b (`Add a goal…`) blew the tier timeout. The cancellation path is structurally identical to Stage 2's confirmation path with one transition difference (resolve as cancelled rather than confirmed; cell falls through to fresh-turn handling on the correcting inbound rather than executing the proposed orchestration). The unit test at `tests/unit/contexts/messaging/application/test_manual_entry_cell.py::test_cancellation_resolves_pending_and_falls_through` covers the exact path. The smoke verdict: substrate-verified by Stage 1+2 plus unit-test coverage of the cancellation transition; live exercise of cancellation defers to a follow-up smoke against a viable inference backend.

- Reflection (executed-evidence discipline at smoke close, prompt 6): The S46 smoke close annotation at `docs/smoke/p13_s46_manual_entry_end_to_end.md` carried executed evidence stage-by-stage; this S47 smoke annotation follows the same discipline. The discipline produces a structurally-honest audit artefact for any procurement-grade reader: the substrate claims at D133/D134/D135/D136 plus the S47 addendum's ThresholdResolver port are not only documented in charter and committed in code, they are operationally executed end-to-end against tenant_a with timestamps, IDs, audit-chain integrity counts, and per-stage verdicts. The smoke document's structural-honesty extends to the closed-with-findings stages — Stage 3+ findings are recorded as findings rather than as failures-of-substrate, with the substrate-verification status separated from the model-viability status.

- Reflection (post-smoke hygiene candidates, prompt 7): Three forward items the smoke surfaces for post-S47 hygiene or a follow-up session: (a) the model-registry-vs-LiteLLM-gateway-routing drift structural test per the captures-entry forward-fix candidate; (b) the per-tenant integration test for PendingClarification lifecycle against the real `pending_clarifications` table (to close the FK-violation gap class at the test boundary); (c) the inference-model-selection strategic block (hosted vs local; per-model tier-timeout; possibly Response B's constrained classification surface). None of these block P14 framing; all defer to either post-S47 hygiene or the next strategic-mode session as appropriate. The S47 substrate ready-for-P14 verdict from the original S47 close (`current-package.md`) holds at smoke close.

methodology: The model-registry-vs-LiteLLM-gateway-routing drift is a first-instance of a class — two configuration surfaces that must stay in sync with no structural test enforcing the invariant. Recurrence test: any future addition to one of the two surfaces (model_registry or gateway model_list) without parallel addition to the other will produce the same failure mode. The qwen2.5:14b hardware-viability finding is also a first-instance — operator dogfooding's latency budget is a load-bearing constraint that the S47 strategic-mode framing did not surface explicitly. The convergence's Response A disposition was made under the assumption that "static-configuration-at-Phase-2-A bumps the model" closes the reliability finding; the smoke evidence shows that the model choice is constrained by hardware as well as by classification accuracy. Both forward to post-S47 hygiene methodology review.

## S47 — UX convergence substrate (multi-turn manual entry cell, dispatch port, confidence-aware composition)
roles: analyst, PM, architect, engineer, technical writer
mode: build session, executing briefs/p13/s47.md

S47 — the UX convergence substrate bridging P13 Wave 1 close and P14 epic framing — landed the convergence's five architectural settlements as charter (D133-D136 plus architecture, principles, deferred-decisions, captures additions) and built the substrate end-to-end across six code commits: the CellDispatch port plus Path A in-process adapter unhitching the Twilio webhook from the LLM call chain; the ConfidenceCalculator port at shared_kernel with the self-reported placeholder adapter plus D130's StructuredOutputParseFailure extension; the PendingClarification domain entity with Postgres persistence and three-stage audit lifecycle (create / resolve / expire); the cell's three-case decision logic plus PendingClarification multi-turn consumption plus WhatsApp shape-aware-clarification rendering; the static configuration bump for REAL_TIME_REQUIRED to `qwen2.5:14b` plus the model registry seed per D133. Seven commits total.

- Produced: charter commits landing D133-D136 plus architecture.md, principles.md, deferred-decisions.md, captures.md, current-package.md, and `briefs/p13/s47.md` (28127d9); CellDispatch port plus InProcessCellDispatchAdapter plus cell-failure logging plus webhook handler refactor plus _SyncCellDispatch test fake (5341c59); ConfidenceCalculator port at `shared_kernel/confidence_calculator.py` plus SelfReportedConfidenceAdapter at `contexts/inference/adapters/` plus StructuredOutputParseFailure typed exception at shared_kernel and LiteLLM adapter (a7163f2); PendingClarification domain entity plus PendingClarificationRepository port plus consumer reader port plus three lifecycle use cases plus Postgres adapter plus Alembic 0021 with partial unique index plus three new permissions (8f7fd02); cell three-case decision logic plus PendingClarification multi-turn consumption plus confidence cut-off configuration at `padhanam/config/messaging.py` plus WhatsApp rendering (df20d9c); REAL_TIME_REQUIRED model pin bump to `qwen2.5:14b` plus model registry seed at `padhanam/config/model_registry.py` with four entries per D133 (4db4476); this commit landing the procedural smoke at `docs/smoke/p13_s47_multi_turn_cell_end_to_end.md` plus this session-log entry plus the current-package S47 close annotation.

- Decisions: D133 (gateway-as-resolution-point plus webhook dispatch port plus model registry), D134 (confidence-aware response composition plus ConfidenceCalculator port plus PendingClarification), D135 (domain-decides-content channel-decides-format rendering pattern), D136 (multi-channel UX architectural primitives). Plus the channel-shaped-rendering principle at `charter/principles.md` Phase 2 principles section. Plus three deferred-decisions entries (multi-channel UX entry updated as activated by D136; shared-kernel CitedResponse structural enforcement entry; cost-aware gateway routing policies entry).

- Tests: ~31 new tests; the full unit-plus-contract run is 1575 passed (+87 from S45 close baseline of 1488, +5 from S46 close), 178 skipped (docker/empty-registry), no failures. 32 of 32 import-linter contracts kept (no new contracts); AST enforcement green. New test files: `tests/unit/apps/api/test_cell_dispatch_inprocess.py` (4 scenarios — dispatch returns quickly, cell runs in background, exception captured + logged with context, concurrent dispatches independent), `tests/unit/shared_kernel/test_confidence_calculator.py` (7 scenarios — self-reported reads response.confidence, missing-value default, default tunability, clamp to [0,1], default range validation, ConfidenceCalculator Protocol conformance), `tests/unit/contexts/messaging/domain/test_pending_clarification.py` (9 scenarios — happy path, six invariant rejections, resolve + expire transitions), `tests/unit/contexts/messaging/application/test_pending_clarification_use_cases.py` (7 scenarios — create persists + audits, create expires prior PENDING, resolve + expire transitions, three authorisation-denial paths). Plus updates to manual_entry_cell unit tests, structured-output parse-failure assertion changes, operator-permission test updates for the three new pending_clarification permissions, latency-tier test for the qwen2.5:14b pin, and ConversationFlow harness stub updates.

- Reflection (convergence settlement integrity, prompt 1): each architectural commitment from D133-D136 landed cleanly in the substrate. The interface-versus-implementation pre-write reconciliation surface 9 caught two draft-time drifts at framing: (a) the brief named `apps/inference/adapters/` as the SelfReportedConfidenceAdapter home, but no such directory exists — the adapter lands at `contexts/inference/adapters/confidence_self_reported.py` (sibling of `outbound/`), recorded at the commit-3 message; (b) the dispatch port shape was named as sync `dispatch(...)` in the brief but the test seam required async-friendly semantics (a test fake awaits the cell synchronously while production schedules a task), so the port is `async def dispatch(...)` — the production adapter still returns promptly via `asyncio.create_task`, the in-flight `await` is trivial. Both shifts preserve the convergence's architectural intent at D133/D134; neither required charter rework.

- Reflection (interface-versus-implementation discipline at draft time, prompt 2): surface 9 was the new pre-write reconciliation surface at S47 framing; it caught both deviations above at commit-draft time rather than at operator review. The discipline addition recorded at the captures methodology entry now has first-instance operational evidence — the framing-time check "am I committing the interface or the implementation?" successfully directed both decisions back to the interface. The CellDispatch port stayed the architectural commitment with InProcessCellDispatchAdapter as the Phase 2-A adapter behind it; the ConfidenceCalculator port stayed the commitment with SelfReportedConfidenceAdapter as the placeholder. Without the surface, the brief's literal paths would have produced an adapter at a nonexistent directory and a port shape that did not test cleanly.

- Reflection (dispatch port and cell-failure logging integration, prompt 3): the bare-`except Exception: pass` at the prior synchronous shape (`apps/api/routers/messaging.py:267`) is closed by the dispatch port's contract. The integration produced no unexpected interactions with the existing Twilio signature verification (the webhook still verifies before dispatch) or the outbound REST API delivery (the cell's `send_message` still runs through the existing path after the dispatch's background task completes). The clean separation comes from the dispatch port carrying the cell-failure logging at *its* contract: callers don't see the cell's exceptions; the structured `cell-run failed in background task` log line emerges with the caller's `context` dict (intake_id, tenant_id, external_id) surfacing as `extra={"context": ...}`. The test for it (`test_dispatch_captures_cell_exception_and_logs`) verifies the contract end-to-end.

- Reflection (PendingClarification audit-chain integrity, prompt 4): the lifecycle events chain cleanly across the multi-turn cascade. The three audit drafts (`messaging.pending_clarification.create / resolve / expire`) follow the same draft-then-recompute shape as `draft_message_event`, so the Postgres audit adapter recomputes `previous_event_hash` and `this_event_hash` inside its locking transaction per D37. Across three lifecycle paths (create→resolve confirmed, create→resolve cancelled, create→expire), the audit chain at the unit-test altitude carries the right shape: one chain-anchoring per lifecycle event, three after-state shapes (creation carries `expires_at` plus `proposed_action_summary`; resolution carries `resolution: confirmed|cancelled` plus `resolved_at`; expiration carries the `resolved_at` only). The procurement-grade defensibility question — can an auditor distinguish "platform acted confidently on a low-confidence guess" from "platform asked for confirmation before acting" — answers yes via the audit chain: every Case 2 emission produces a `create` event, and every confirmed action carries a `resolve` event immediately before the portfolio write. Phase 2-A operator dogfooding will exercise the chain at live volume.

- Reflection (confidence-aware composition operator-facing reality, prompt 5): the smoke is procedural; the operator-facing UX records live. The shape-aware clarification at Case 2 reads as a suggestion-as-question per principle pattern 2 (`I think you want to <action>. Is that right? (yes / no)`). The hex-citation line per D131 Shape 1 holds on the confirming-reply confirmation. Whether the cut-off pair (0.8 / 0.5) hits the right balance — too many Case 1 false confidences feel pushy, too many Case 2 clarifications feel pedantic — is calibration that emerges with dogfooding data; the substrate is in place to tune without code change. The cut-offs threaded through MessagingSettings means a smoke-time recalibration is `MESSAGING_CONFIDENCE_HIGH_CUTOFF=0.85` away.

- Reflection (static config bump validation, prompt 6): the bump from `qwen2.5:7b` to `qwen2.5:14b` is a substantive Phase 2-A reliability commitment, not a cosmetic config nudge. The S46 smoke surfaced 0/7 AddDataPointIntent classifications across four phrasings at the 7B model; the 14B variant has substantially stronger discriminative capability on structured-output tasks per published benchmarks. Whether the bump closes the operational problem in the operator's actual deployment requires the live smoke — if `qwen2.5:14b` also misses AddDataPointIntent under the operator's hardware (or under load), Response A's static-configuration disposition has not closed the convergence's concern 5, and the operator should override to a hosted alternative (gpt-4o-mini, claude-haiku-4-5) via `INFERENCE_REAL_TIME_REQUIRED_MODEL`. The model registry at `padhanam/config/model_registry.py` carries the four candidate models with cost-per-call so the audit chain records which model fired and procurement-grade defensibility holds across the bump. The smoke's stage-2 medium-confidence cascade is the empirical test for `qwen2.5:14b`'s classification accuracy; if every attempt classifies at confidence ≥ 0.8, the cell skips Case 2 entirely and the convergence's principle-2-binding surface stays untested in practice.

- Reflection (methodology surfaces, prompt 7): the interface-versus-implementation methodology candidate (promotion-ready at the captures entry from this session's charter commit) earned its first operational instance during S47's commit-draft cycles, as recorded above. No additional methodology candidates from S47 itself warrant captures entries — the substrate work largely followed the established patterns (consumer ports per S46 precedent; intake-canonical orchestration per S44b; audit-event drafts per S45). One observation worth tracking but not yet promotion-threshold: the cell now consumes seven dependencies at construction (structured_output_port, portfolio_gateway, actor, confidence_calculator, pending_clarification_reader, pending_clarification_repository, audit_port plus two cutoffs). Phase 2-B+ ConversationFlow implementers landing in P14 with similar dependency counts will trigger the question of whether a CellComposition value object captures the cross-cutting infrastructure separately from per-cell deps. Single instance; not a captures entry.

methodology: Surface 9 (interface-versus-implementation discipline) — the captures-promotion-ready candidate's *first operational application* — caught two draft-time drifts at framing (the apps/inference/adapters/ nonexistent path and the sync-vs-async dispatch port shape). The candidate's promotion at the captures entry has structurally-honest evidence the discipline does load-bearing work at S47, supporting Phase 2-A close hygiene's methodology-document promotion path. The four-instance evidence base from the convergence conversation plus the two S47 framing instances brings the candidate to six-instance pattern with two distinct surfaces (charter-altitude drafting at the convergence; code-altitude drafting at S47).

## S46 smoke — manual entry cell live-stack verification
roles: engineer, technical writer
mode: strategic-mode-continuation (operational execution evidence continuing the S46 close)

The S46 manual entry cell (D115 first ConversationFlow implementer, D122 latency-tier routing, D132 four-layer model ontology) verified end-to-end against tenant_a on the rebuilt `padhanam-api` image (digest `sha256:523e9419…`, pin advanced from S45's `sha256:77ad47bf…`) across the operator-WhatsApp-to-portfolio-state cascade. Two CreateCaseIntent cascades clean (stages 1 and 8e-i); seven AddDataPointIntent attempts across four phrasings all classified to UnclearIntent (the cell behaved contract-correctly throughout — clarification, no write, no citation line); two structural-honesty findings surfaced and captured at `log/captures.md` rather than fixed in-session.

- Produced: Three commits across the smoke-execution arc. `chore(compose)` `padhanam-api` image re-pin to `sha256:523e9419…` after `make build-api` rebuilt the image to carry S46 code (the running container had been S45's `sha256:77ad47bf…`); `docs(charter)` two captures entries at `log/captures.md` (webhook synchronous-cell-run-vs-Twilio-timeout finding with cold-vs-warm latency paragraph; intent-extraction reliability at REAL_TIME_REQUIRED with `qwen2.5:7b` finding with instrumentation-gap paragraph); `docs(p13/s46-smoke)` landing the executed-evidence smoke document, this session-log entry, and the current-package smoke-complete annotation.

- Smoke outcome: two CreateCase cascades (stage 1 Case `fa6401b0` "Q3 portfolio review"; stage 8e-i Case `4fac3ae0` "Q3 budget review" against the now-similar-named existing case, no conflation); one AddDataPoint attempt at 8a using the smoke document's own template phrasing produced UnclearIntent; three structural rephrasings exercising distinct surface-cue patterns (verb-first-full-title, target-front-loaded, no-verb-noun-phrase) each sent twice (six runs total) all produced byte-identical UnclearIntent clarifications. Final tenant_a state: intakes 17 (+11 from baseline 6), cases 6 (+2), data_points 3 (unchanged — no Add succeeded), assertions 6 (unchanged), messages 21 (+18), tenant_audit 165 (+31). Audit chain holds: 165 events, exactly 1 chain entry point (genesis), 165 distinct `this_event_hash` values (zero duplicates), 0 broken links. D122/D132 trace attributes verified at the stage-1 LLM-call observation: all four `gen_ai.model.*` dimensions present (provider=ollama, account=default, version=qwen2.5:7b, configuration=`latency_tier=real_time_required;temperature=0.0;structured_output_schema=present`), scope `padhanam.inference.litellm`. D131 citation contract held in all 9 outbound replies (2 cited per Shape 1 short-hex-plus-timestamp; 7 clarifications carrying no citation line).

- Decisions: No new D-entries. The smoke produces operational evidence for existing commitments (D115, D122, D128, D129, D130, D131, D132) plus two structural-honesty findings preserved at `log/captures.md` (the webhook-contract violation finding feeds a future build session for Path A in-process asyncio dispatch plus cell-failure logging; the intent-extraction reliability finding is first-class convergence-session input).

- Tests: No test surface changed — operational execution evidence. The S46 build-session suite (`tests/unit`, `tests/contract`, `tests/integration/api` green at close) stands.

- Reflection (LLM cold-vs-warm latency dynamic, prompt 1): the synchronous-LLM-vs-Twilio-timeout finding surfaced at stage 1 (LLM call 22.95s, ngrok status 0) — initially framed as "the 23s LLM blows Twilio's 15s webhook timeout, retry-duplication risk." The round-2 rephrasings produced a richer empirical picture: the same LLM call ran at 18.62s (cold after 13min idle), 9.07s (warm 35s later), 8.05s (warm 18s later). Ollama's 5-min keep-alive unloads the model on idle; the next call pays cold-start cost. The webhook-contract finding holds on grounds independent of which side of the timeout a given run falls (a synchronous handler is wrong on contract grounds even at 8s — it ties up a server worker for the full duration and writes its response to an already-closed connection), but the empirical urgency calibrates by usage pattern. The captures entry now incorporates both endpoints honestly.

- Reflection (intent-extraction reliability and N=1-vs-N=4 catch, prompt 2): stage 8a's AddDataPointIntent attempt classified to UnclearIntent using the smoke document's own template phrasing. The initial disposition drafting in the cross-thread captures wording overclaimed three rephrasings as "having run" before they actually ran — Claude Code caught the N=1-versus-N=4 conflation at captures-entry drafting time and surfaced the discrepancy; the operator chose Path 1+2 (actually run the rephrasings then close) over Path 2 (close at N=1 with softer wording). The rephrasings ran (verb-first, target-front-loaded, no-verb-noun-phrase; each sent twice for six runs total); all classified to UnclearIntent. Phrasing-sensitivity empirically falsified; intent-class blindness on AddDataPoint at `qwen2.5:7b` / REAL_TIME_REQUIRED is the surviving claim, supported by N=4 distinct phrasings across 7 runs. Three architectural responses for the convergence session captured: raise the REAL_TIME_REQUIRED tier model (Response A), constrain the classification surface or expand the prompt (Response B), render model uncertainty as user-facing clarification more honestly (Response C). Phase 2-A operator-dogfooding-via-WhatsApp viability opens as a first-class convergence input rather than mid-stream optimisation.

- Reflection (D131 first-instance operator-facing reality, prompt 3): the cited replies in both CreateCase cascades carried the Shape 1 line cleanly — `— ref fa6401b0 · intake 85622595 · 21:45 UTC` and `— ref 4fac3ae0 · intake 583c7c36 · 22:30 UTC`. The short-hex prefixes match the persisted IDs exactly. The citation line sat below a blank line beneath the confirmation prose, scanning as a single short line. The clarification-path replies (8a + rephrasings) carried no citation line — D131 contract held across all 7 clarification cases. Whether operators find the citations useful, intrusive, or ignorable at any volume is convergence-session-relevant; the smoke confirms the rendering shape without stressing operator preference.

- Reflection (instrumentation gap on prompt/output capture, prompt 4): the Langfuse trace captures the D122/D132 dimensions and token counts but does not capture prompt content or model output. The smoke cannot distinguish whether `qwen2.5:7b` returned clean UnclearIntent JSON (the model knows it doesn't know) or whether `parse_intent` coerced malformed output to UnclearIntent (the model produced something Padhanam can't parse). The two cases have different architectural implications for confidence-aware composition. Folded as a paragraph inside the intent-extraction reliability captures entry rather than as a separate finding because the gap is structurally linked to the intent-extraction finding's analytical surface.

- Reflection (discipline-working signals during smoke execution, prompt 5): three discipline-working signals at this smoke worth indexing for Phase 2-A close hygiene methodology assessment. First, the N=1-versus-N=4 catch at captures-entry drafting where the build-execution surface caught a strategic-altitude overclaim before it landed in repo state — pre-write reconciliation at a non-traditional surface (cross-thread captures wording rather than within-brief acceptance criteria). Second, the cold-vs-warm latency finding emerging late through the round-2 rephrasings rather than at the initial stage-1 single data point — empirical evidence can sharpen mid-smoke if the test surface generates richer measurement, even when not planned. Third, three actually-distinct phrasings exercising different surface-cue patterns producing falsification evidence rather than three near-variants producing redundant noise — phrasing-pattern variation as a test-design discipline at the smoke surface. All three are convergence-input-shaping signals; not promoted to methodology entries at this commit because single-instance observations.

methodology: The three discipline-working signals (pre-write reconciliation across surfaces; mid-smoke empirical sharpening from richer measurement; phrasing-pattern variation as test-design discipline) are observation-level recordings, not promotion-threshold-crossing recurrence. The N=1-versus-N=4 catch is structurally similar to the pre-write reconciliation pattern at brief altitude (Phase 1's pre-write reconciliation pattern at 19+ instances), but the surface (cross-thread captures wording) is novel — worth tracking whether the surface recurs at Phase 2-A close methodology assessment. The cold-vs-warm finding's late-emergence shape recurs at the third-instance threshold if Phase 2-A smoke sessions produce more empirical-sharpening-mid-smoke instances.

## S46 — Manual entry cell (first ConversationFlow implementer) plus latency-tier routing plus four-layer model ontology
roles: analyst, PM, architect, engineer, technical writer
mode: build session, executing briefs/p13/s46.md

S46 — the P13 Wave 1 end-to-end exercise — landed the manual entry cell as the first ConversationFlow implementer (D115), the D122 latency-tier routing, and the D132 four-layer model ontology, end-to-end from a WhatsApp message through structured-output intent extraction, target identifier resolution, and an intake-canonical portfolio write to a cited confirmation rendered back to WhatsApp. Twelve commits. P13 Wave 1 closes at S46.

- Produced: charter updates landing D132 plus the D131 first-instance Expected-exercise update (14e7d0e); intent value objects with the four-variant discriminated union (9dd832f); the target identifier resolution helper (5b162d0); the four-layer model ontology and LatencyTier at `shared_kernel/inference.py` (1f7033b); the InferenceSettings `latency_tier_config` extension (3f40c32); the tier-aware LiteLLM adapter plus the `model_ontology` sibling plus the call-site retrofit (f257e14); the PortfolioGateway consumer port plus the apps/-layer adapter (7351911); the manual entry cell plus `cell_response` (a49a34a); the ConversationFlow harness registration (64bf0d8); the composition-root wiring plus end-to-end integration tests (fad90d7); the procedural live-stack smoke (0977ca9); this commit landing the session-log entry and the P13 Wave 1 close marker.

- Decisions: D132 (four-layer model ontology at the inference port). D131's Expected-exercise line updated for the S46 first-instance exercise. No other new D-entries — the cell, the intent value objects, the resolution helper, and the latency-tier routing land under existing commitments (D115 ConversationFlow, D122 latency-tier, D129 messaging, D130 structured output, D131 provenance).

- Tests: roughly 55 new and updated tests; `tests/unit`, `tests/contract`, and `tests/integration/api` green; 32 of 32 import-linter contracts kept; AST enforcement green. The ConversationFlow contract harness's five conformance scenarios — dormant at S45 with an empty registry — now run against the manual entry cell as the first registered implementer.

- Reflection (pre-write reconciliation, prompt 1): nine surfaces. Five absorbed mechanically (D-number, ActorContext synthesis, the three intake orchestration signatures, CLI-out-of-scope, type-identifier search). Four were structural-honesty findings, all operator-approved before code: (A) the brief's Path B — a *required* tier parameter — contradicted D122's committed "Phase 1 call sites preserve current behaviour with opt-in retrofit", resolved to Path A (a defaulted `latency_tier`); (B) the cell could not sit at the domain layer the brief named — a cell holding ports breaches the `layers-messaging` hexagonal contract — and moved to the application layer; (C) "ModelIdentifier at the call signature" was wrong in kind (call sites carry no Provider/Account knowledge, the Configuration layer overlaps existing request fields), resolved to adapter-boundary composition; (D) surface 8's consumer-port shape settled to a single `PortfolioGateway` carrying both the resolution reads and the orchestration writes, and surface 9's call-site count (four) confirmed Path A was tractable. The cleanest reconciliation cycle of P13 — four structural findings, none of which reached a code commit.

- Reflection (methodology candidate, prompt 2): S46 Item 4 verification found that "four-layer model ontology" (D132) is *new* substrate, distinct from the pre-existing "four-layer constraint stack" at `architecture.md` (D80) — two unrelated "four-layer" concepts. The verification did real work (it caught the distinction before the brief drafted against the wrong one) but produced new substrate, not recurrence of the "prose already landed" pattern. The framing-conversation pre-write-reconciliation methodology candidate stays at two-instance evidence (S44 Item 4, S45 Item 4) for the P13-close hygiene assessment.

- Reflection (D131 first-instance, prompt 3): the citation discipline felt natural at the cell altitude — `CellResponse` carries the three citation tuples, the cell populates `cited_intake_records` and `cited_artefacts` from the orchestration-result IDs, and `render_for_whatsapp` renders the compact Shape-1 line. The one honest gap: `cited_audit_events` stays empty because the intake-owned write-result DTOs (`CaseWriteResult`, `DataPointWriteResult`) do not surface audit-event IDs. The convention-versus-structural-enforcement gap is real but caused no implementation friction — the cell simply cites what it holds in scope. Recorded at `charter/captures.md` for the Phase 3 close audit.

- Reflection (ConversationFlow harness, prompt 4): the harness's five scenarios fit the cell cleanly — no contract-scenario gaps. `make_instance` needed offline stubs (a structured-output stub returning an UnclearIntent extraction so a harness `turn` runs without an LLM and never reaches the gateway); the `@runtime_checkable` `isinstance` check and the open/turn/close lifecycle held without adjustment. One genuine adjustment: S45's `test_harness_is_ready_at_s45` asserted an *empty* registry — that was an S45-state test, not a permanent contract, and it was replaced with `test_manual_entry_cell_is_registered`. Second-instance evidence for the harness machinery design: it absorbed the first implementer with no harness change.

- Reflection (D132 Configuration scope, prompt 5): the Configuration layer settled to a *narrower* scope than "everything affecting the call." It carries `latency_tier`, `temperature`, `max_tokens`, and the structured-output schema — the per-call *parameters* — but not the system prompt or message list, which already live first-class on the request surface. Finding C drove this: a Configuration layer that re-homed existing request fields would create two homes per field. `ModelIdentifier` composes at the LiteLLM adapter boundary and `audit_dimensions()` captures the four `gen_ai.model.*` span attributes per call; the audit capture worked cleanly in the adapter unit tests.

- Reflection (latency-tier operational evidence, prompt 6): the S46 smoke is procedural — the build environment cannot reach docker or the Twilio Sandbox — so the per-tier latency baseline records at the operator's live execution. The cell's only LLM call is the `REAL_TIME_REQUIRED` intent extraction (a small structured generation); no `ASYNC_TOLERANT` call fires on the cell path. The two evaluation appliers opt into `ASYNC_TOLERANT` per D122's substrate-analysis classification; the agent loop and `request_completion` stay on the `REAL_TIME_REQUIRED` default — both are user-invoked surfaces. S45's 97 ms inbound cascade remains the reference for the webhook leg.

- Reflection (convergence-session activation, prompt 7): P13 Wave 1 closes at S46. The UX convergence strategic-mode session named in the S45 `charter/captures.md` entry — combining multi-channel UX, provenance-aware response composition, and confidence-aware response composition into one coherent design — activates now, between this close and P14 epic framing. It is the next strategic-mode block.

methodology: Finding A is the brief-conflicts-with-a-committed-D-entry pattern recurring — the framing brief's Path-B disposition was reasoned from the call-site count without cross-checking D122's committed text. The S45 messaging substrate (D129), structured-output discipline (D130), and ConversationFlow shape (D115) verified end-to-end against tenant_a on the rebuilt `padhanam-api` image — including a real Twilio WhatsApp Sandbox round trip in both directions. - Smoke outcome: all ten stages green. Stage 0 — Twilio Sandbox setup plus the ngrok-binding methodology-gap fix. Stage 1 — `make build-api` plus `make migrate` (0019/0020 on both tenant planes; the `messages` table and the `WHATSAPP_INBOUND` enum extension verified). Stage 2 — outbound via LocalEcho (Message `2cb6d146`, `external_id=local-echo-…`, DELIVERED, no Twilio call). Stage 3 — outbound via Twilio (Message `97e5d4c5`, `external_id=SM81b3ed…`, QUEUED, delivered to the operator's WhatsApp). Stage 4 — inbound webhook: a real Twilio WhatsApp message produced IntakeRecord `b165f334` (WHATSAPP_INBOUND) then Message `c4ae90e6` (INBOUND, `intake_id` linking), 97 ms cascade. Stage 5 — invalid signature rejected 403, zero side effects, `AUTH_FAILURE` security event. Stages 6-7 — GET routes plus cross-tenant 404s. Stage 8 — audit chain: 134 events, one genesis entry point, zero broken links, zero duplicate hashes. Stage 9 — pagination plus direction/channel filters.

- Decisions: No new D-entries. The smoke produces operational evidence for existing commitments (D119, D128, D129, D130).

- Tests: No test surface changed — operational execution evidence. The S45 build-session suite (`tests/unit` plus `tests/contract` green at close) stands.

- Reflection (LocalEcho operator-dev-cycle, prompt 1): LocalEcho's substrate-depth dividend is real and bounded. It removed the external dependency for substrate iteration — route, persistence, and the audit chain are verifiable in-process with no credentials and no Twilio call — but it cannot verify actual WhatsApp delivery. - Reflection (methodology recurrence, prompt 4): the smoke-document stage-0 ngrok-binding gap — the original draft named `ngrok http 8000` without verifying that port 8000 is host-bound (it is not, per S5's only-Caddy-binds rule). Brief-vs-codebase-actuality drift at the smoke-document altitude — the same shape as the SMS-vs-WhatsApp finding at the S45 brief, scaled down to a procedural concern. Recurrence evidence; deferred to the Phase 2-A close methodology assessment per the philosophical pruning discipline.

- Reflection (cascade latency, prompt 5): stage 4's Twilio-POST-to-200-response measured 97 ms upstream. The inbound cascade — signature verification, dual-decorator authorisation, IntakeRecord plus Message persistence, two audit events — sits well inside a real-time latency budget.   - Commit 8 (`feat`, 7cf4295) `shared_kernel/conversation_flow.py` (ConversationFlow Protocol plus five value objects) and `shared_kernel/structured_output.py` (the structured-output discipline).
  - Commit 9 (`feat`, 8161ce5) the inference adapter's additive `generate_structured` method implementing StructuredOutputPort.
  - Commit 10 (`test`, ccbaabc) the ConversationFlow and StructuredOutputPort contract harnesses.

- Decisions: D129, D130, D131 landed at commit 1. D129 references the existing D119 for the WhatsApp channel and Twilio vendor commitments rather than restating them — the central pre-write-reconciliation correction (the original brief's "D119 messaging substrate" collided with D119's existing WhatsApp-via-Twilio decision; the updated brief reassigned to D129/D130/D131). D131 commits an architectural posture (provenance-aware response composition) that no Phase 2-A surface exercises; the substrate-depth-overrides-YAGNI justification is on the record for the Phase 3 close audit.

- Tests: `tests/unit` plus `tests/contract` green at session close — 1488 passed, 183 skipped, pytest exit 0. The skips are the docker-dependent tenant-isolation suites plus the five ConversationFlow contract scenarios (parametrised over the empty registry — no implementer at S45). Roughly 104 new tests across the S45 surface: 14 Message domain, 12 messaging tenant-isolation (skip-without-docker), 18 delivery-adapter, 10 messaging use-case, 5 inbound-orchestration, 15 shared-kernel (ConversationFlow plus structured-output), 10 inference structured-output, 12 contract-harness, 7 messaging HTTP integration, plus the intake-source domain test. The two pre-existing environmental failures carried from S44a/S44b did not recur in this docker-less run.

- Enforcement layer: import-linter grew from 29 to 32 contracts — `layers-messaging` (the hexagonal layer order for the new context) plus `twilio-confined` and `twilio-confined-apps` (the vendor-SDK confinement pair mirroring litellm/langfuse/neo4j; `import twilio` resolves only inside `contexts/messaging/adapters/outbound/twilio/`). `contexts.messaging.*` was added to the three cross-context independence contracts, the domain-purity contract, and the litellm/langfuse/neo4j confinement source lists. AST enforcement (`test_dockerfile_workspace_coverage`) holds — messaging is a workspace member with its Dockerfile COPY directive. The orchestration placement at the intake context (D127 alternative (d)) keeps the cross-context contracts clean: the `MessageWriter` consumer port at `contexts/intake/application/ports/` imports nothing from `contexts/messaging`.

- Pre-write reconciliation: the eight surfaces were verified in the prior framing cycle and dispositioned into the updated brief (SMS → WhatsApp; D-numbers → D129/D130/D131; orchestration at intake per D127(d); import-linter count → 32; CLI deferred; DTO/query paths → `apps/api/routers/`; the `Message` name reused across bounded contexts; `twilio-confined` pair added). Session-open verification found the working tree clean and HEAD unchanged at `e322dc4` — nothing had shifted since the disposition conversation. One new finding surfaced mid-build: the twilio SDK was neither installed nor in the lockfile, so messaging became the first context since retrieval_evaluation to carry its own `pyproject.toml` — the per-context manifest is the vendor-dep-scoping mechanism, and messaging genuinely needs to scope `twilio` (optimization/portfolio/intake skipped a manifest because they added no new third-party dep). One path correction: the brief said `charter/captures.md`; the file is `log/captures.md`.

- methodology (line 1): **Brief-vs-existing-D-entry drift as a distinct reconciliation class.** The original S45 brief said "SMS" throughout and proposed "D119" for the messaging substrate — but D119 was already the WhatsApp-via-Twilio channel decision, and the P13 epic commits WhatsApp. This is neither brief-path-drift (file naming) nor brief-vs-domain-model drift (type identifiers) nor as-built-versus-as-framed drift — it is the brief contradicting an *existing committed D-entry*. The mitigation is the same pre-write reconciliation discipline but the surface is the decisions.md tail: a brief that names a D-number or a channel/vendor must reconcile against the actual decisions.md state. Caught cleanly in the prior cycle; the updated brief absorbed the correction. Second-instance evidence for the framing-conversation pre-write reconciliation methodology candidate at a new substrate (decisions.md as the reconciliation surface).

- methodology (line 2): **The new protocol-selection principles did load-bearing analytical work at first application.** D129's protocol disposition — direct Twilio Python SDK at the messaging adapter, Twilio MCP server rejected on Alpha-stage vendor-readiness with a named re-evaluation trigger — maps directly onto principles.md lines 22-23 (scenario-driven protocol selection; architectural commitments evolve). The principle supplied the analytical frame: "service-to-service runtime integration defaults to vendor SDK, modulated by vendor readiness." Without it the disposition would have read as an ad-hoc justification; with it, it is a principled application carrying an explicit re-evaluation trigger. First-instance evidence that principles.md lines 22-23 produce real reasoning value, not ceremony.

- methodology (line 3): **The intake-canonical commitment generalised cleanly across context-pairs.** D128's second-instance test was whether the intake-then-X orchestration shape holds when the downstream context is not portfolio. `record_intake_and_record_inbound_message` is a field-for-field analogue of `record_intake_and_create_case` — same dual-decorator shape, same `record_intake`-then-consumer-port structure, same `intake_id` propagation — with a `MessageWriter` consumer port replacing `PortfolioWriter`. D127 alternative (d) pre-settled the placement, so the "critical framing question" the original brief posed was already charter-resolved. The consumer-port pattern is altitude- and context-pair-agnostic.

- methodology (line 4): **A permission-set change ripples to every enumeration site — a type-altitude drift the build did not catch until the session-close run.** Commit 5 grew the operator authorisation_set from eight to twelve but did not update the two tests that hard-assert the exact set; the full `tests/unit`-plus-`tests/contract` run at session close surfaced both failures (commit 13 corrected them). This is a variant of the S44a brief-vs-domain-model drift candidate at the value altitude: a change to an *enumerated set* (the operator's permissions) must update every site that asserts the set's membership, and a grep for the changed constant's siblings at change time would have caught it. The discipline addition: when a use case or policy adds to an enumerated collection, grep for existing exact-set assertions before the commit closes.

- methodology (line 5): **Vendor-SDK-plus-local-development-adapter as a named pattern.** The Twilio adapter (production) plus the LocalEcho adapter (local-first development, no credentials) behind one `MessageDeliveryPort`, selected by `MESSAGING_ADAPTER`, parallels the LiteLLM-gateway-plus-Ollama-default precedent (D4/D15) and the structural shape of vendor-flexibility (D27/D30). The pattern: a vendor adapter and a local-first adapter implementing the same port, with configuration selecting between them, so the local-first principle holds without the vendor account. First-instance naming; the recurrence test fires at the next external-vendor integration (P14 calendar/email).

- Reflection — LocalEcho substrate-depth (brief prompt 5): the LocalEcho adapter carries unit-test coverage and is the configured `MESSAGING_ADAPTER` default; the live-stack smoke names it the default development path at stage 2. Whether it is exercised in a genuine operator dev cycle (the laptop stack without a Twilio account) awaits the operator running the smoke — docker was not reachable from the build environment. The adapter is not inert: it is the default selection and the messaging HTTP integration tests run the send path against a delivery double, so the LocalEcho substrate is load-bearing for local development rather than speculative.

- Reflection — D131 first-instance accountability (brief prompt 6): no Phase 2-A surface implements a ConversationFlow consumer, so D131 (provenance-aware response composition) commits an architectural posture S45 does not exercise. Recorded honestly: the substrate-depth-overrides-YAGNI commitment is on the record; first exercise lands at P14 when the audit-conversation and mirror-conversation ConversationFlow implementers land; verification is a Phase 3 close audit input.

## 2026-05-22 — Charter principles addition — protocol selection plus evolution
roles: technical writer
mode: strategic (charter commit; no code changes; strategic-conversation outcome appending two principles to `charter/principles.md`)

Strategic-conversation outcome from the Phase 2-A S45 framing approach. Two new principles append to the Architectural section of `charter/principles.md`, extending the existing line 21 meta-position (committed standards require demonstrated cross-vendor consolidation). The first makes protocol selection scenario-driven and vendor-readiness-modulated: agent-to-tool integration defaults to MCP, service-to-service runtime integration defaults to vendor SDK, cross-vendor LLM abstraction is via LiteLLM, observability is via OpenTelemetry — and vendor readiness at the chosen protocol determines whether the scenario's architectural default is the actual framing-time disposition. The second makes architectural commitments explicitly evolving rather than permanent, and names the operational commitment — ongoing port-level verification (contract harnesses, configuration-driven swap tests, vendor-agnostic integration evidence) — that keeps "built to evolve" real rather than aspirational. The conversation surfaced from the S45 messaging substrate's MCP-versus-direct framing question; the Twilio MCP server's Alpha status and AI-coding-agent design orientation made the case for direct vendor SDK at the messaging adapter, which generalised into the two principles. S45 framing inherits the principles as reference points for the Item 1 protocol-choice surface.

- Produced: One commit (`docs(charter)`, this commit) — two new principles appended to the Architectural section of `charter/principles.md` immediately after the line 21 protocol-commitments bullet; this session log entry. The commit is referenced by its conventional-commit message rather than a SHA per the same-commit-self-reference discipline (pre-Step-7 hygiene, 2026-05-20).
- Decisions: No new D-entries. The principles extend the existing line 21 meta-position; they make the scenario-driven default and the evolution discipline explicit without selecting between alternatives.
- Tests: None. Documentation-only change.
