## 2026-05-21 — Charter-export tooling commit (parallel-block close before S43)
roles: engineer, technical writer, analyst
mode: build (tooling commit; closes parallel-block work surfaced at pre-P13 hygiene close)

- Produced: One substantive commit plus this session log entry.
  - Commit c3f5040 (`feat(scripts/charter-export)`): allowlist-based charter snapshot tooling at `scripts/charter-export.py` reading `docs/charter-archive-manifest.md` as single source of truth; `Makefile` `charter-export` target; manifest pending-confirmation items resolved per operator positions (`p13-epic.md` kept; `framing-landing-commit.md` kept; `s43.md` added with deferred drop at S43 close) and the "Pending operator confirmation" section removed; manifest file-count tally corrected to 48. Dry-run and live-run verified at 48 files; live-run produced `charter-20260521-1403/` plus `charter-20260521-1403.zip`, both git-ignored.
  - Commit this commit (`docs(p13)`): this session log entry.

- Decisions: No new D-entries. Tooling commit; no architectural commitment.

- Tests: None. Tooling commit; dry-run plus live-run verify the script behaves; no test-suite extension.

- Pre-write reconciliation: Five surfaces verified before commit 1. Surface 1 found `scripts/` is a pre-existing directory (seven P9-P11 live-stack smoke scripts), not new as the brief framed it — commit-body paragraph 3 and reflection prompt 3 were rewritten to the honest framing on operator disposition. Surface 2's dry-run reported 47 files (48 after the `s43.md` allowlist addition), not the brief's 45/46 — the manifest tally and the brief both carried an arithmetic error (the component breakdown summed to 47); manifest tally corrected to 48 and `.gitignore` confirmed `/*.zip` is the existing repo-root pattern. Surface 3 found `.gitignore` already covers all three output patterns — no `.gitignore` edit; the commit landed three files, not four. Surfaces 4 (manifest pending-items) and 5 (no scope creep) were as expected. Two operator dispositions resolved in prose ahead of commit 1.

- Reflection prompts answered:

  1. *Parallel-block-converges as session shape.* This session's work shape — parallel work the operator builds incrementally outside the session sequence, converging into a single commit when ready — is structurally distinct from the four session shapes named in `charter/methodology.md` (substrate, bridge, transport, hygiene). It is not substrate (no bounded context or domain layer), not bridge (no verification-and-hygiene gap between two build sessions), not transport (no HTTP surface), and not cleanly hygiene (hygiene closes accumulated carryover or drift; this closes a discrete capability built in parallel). The closest precedent is the brand-guidelines-plus-`tokens.css` block discussed at the S30b conversation: parallel work converging into a commit when ready. This is the first instance to land with a session-log entry that names the shape explicitly. Whether it earns a fifth shape name (parallel-block-close) or absorbs under hygiene at the lighter end is a second-instance question; the distinguishing property worth tracking is the convergence pattern — artefacts accumulate in the working tree outside the session sequence, then land as a micro-block when the operator judges them ready, rather than being scoped into a brief up front. Recurrence test fires at the next parallel-block convergence; promotion threshold at second instance.

  2. *Manifest pending-items as commit-time discipline.* The manifest was drafted with three open items parked in a "Pending operator confirmation" section at its tail rather than blocking the draft until every allowlist decision was settled. The pattern carries audit-trail value: the draft captured what was genuinely uncertain at draft time (two files added beyond the original spec, one held out), the operator's resolution landed at commit time, and the draft-uncertainty → resolution trail is legible. The alternatives are worse — blocking the draft slows the parallel-block work, and silently folding the uncertain items in hides the judgement call. The discipline's shape: park genuinely-open questions visibly in the artefact, resolve at commit time, then migrate the resolved audit trail into the commit message and session log so the artefact itself does not carry permanent low-value pending-state content (the "Pending operator confirmation" section was deleted at this commit for exactly that reason). First-instance observation; recurrence test fires at the next document drafted with open questions at its tail.

  3. *scripts/ widens from smoke-only to smoke-plus-repo-discipline.* The directory has carried live-stack smoke-verification scripts (`smoke_p9_s32.py` through `smoke_p11_s42.py`) since P9. `charter-export.py` is the first non-smoke entry, extending the directory's scope to repo-discipline tooling. The two-altitude convention itself (`ops/` as importable runtime package; `scripts/` as standalone-executable directory) is pre-existing and stable; this commit widens what `scripts/` carries, not what altitudes exist. The widening is mechanically clean (standalone executable, no package import contract change), so the directory's structural contract held without modification. Methodology observation candidate: the two-altitude convention deserves documentation at a future hygiene window if a third widening or third altitude surfaces; without documentation, the next tooling addition risks landing under the wrong altitude. Not promoted to a binding commitment at this session; recurrence test fires at the next tooling addition.

  4. *Brief-authoring-time verification gap — triple-instance evidence.* The pre-write reconciliation discipline has now caught a brief-asserted factual error about repo state in each of the last three sessions. P13 framing landing: the brief asserted the D116/D117/D118 architecture-primitive numbers without verifying against current `charter/decisions.md` and `charter/architecture.md`; reconciliation caught the D-number scramble. Pre-P13 hygiene: the brief asserted four field shapes from P12 audit-finding snapshots without verifying against current code; reconciliation caught four divergences. This session: the brief asserted `scripts/` as a new directory and inherited a 45-file tally without verifying against repo state or the breakdown's own arithmetic; reconciliation caught both. The pattern generalises from D-numbers to any factual claim about repo state. The P13 framing landing methodology line set a recurrence test at P14 framing; this session fires that test early and in a broader shape — the gap is not D-reference-specific. The structural reading: strategic-mode brief-authoring either verifies factual claims about repo state against available sources (project knowledge for charter content; explicit pre-write-reconciliation marking for working-tree state a Claude.ai conversation cannot reach), or carries the assertion as a known-to-need-verification marker rather than as a bare fact. The current bare-fact pattern produces structural-honesty findings reliably — the downstream reconciliation works — but it pushes verification cost to the commit session, where reversal is more expensive (a false claim in a drafted brief becomes a false claim in a commit body unless caught). Charter methodology promotion candidate at the next hygiene window; this is the strongest of the three instances, because it is the third and because it shows the gap is general rather than D-reference-specific.

- methodology (line 1): **Parallel-block-close as a candidate fifth session shape.** Parallel work accumulated in the working tree outside the session sequence, landed as a micro-block when ready — distinct from substrate, bridge, transport, and hygiene. First instance named in a session log; see reflection prompt 1. Promotion threshold at second instance.

- methodology (line 2): **The brief-authoring-time verification gap is triple-instance evidenced and general.** Strategic-mode briefs assert factual claims about repo state without verification; pre-write reconciliation has caught one such error per session across the last three sessions, spanning D-numbers, code field-shapes, and directory/arithmetic state. Charter methodology promotion candidate at the next hygiene window; see reflection prompt 4.

- **Charter-export tooling committed; working tree clean entering S43.**

## 2026-05-21 — Pre-P13 hygiene — schema.md formalisation plus old-platform-name find-replace
roles: technical writer, analyst
mode: build (P13 pre-substrate hygiene; closes two carryover items before S43 opens)

- Produced: Six commits closed the session — five per the brief's planned shape, plus one corrective after the post-session verification grep.
  - Commit e1067ee (`docs(charter)`): schema.md "Cross-cutting binding shapes" H2 section with three sub-sections (TenantContext, ErrorResponse, canonical cursor codec), field tables, and the cross-cutting-shape audit-trail role mirroring the database-table sections.
  - Commit 1eea111 (`docs(p13/hygiene)`): old-platform-name find-replace across three non-allowlist files (`charter/methodology.md`, `charter/packages.md`, `charter/packages/p13-epic.md`); post-edit `(Quorum|Zephyr)` grep returns only allowlist matches plus the Q1-exempt dated snapshot.
  - Commit 43c3603 (`docs(charter)`): phase-2-audit-inputs.md "Phase 2-A close hygiene list" section with the roadmap stale-headers entry as first item.
  - Commit fe18198 (`docs(charter)`): current-package.md pre-P13 hygiene close marker; pre-hygiene prompt preserved verbatim at `briefs/p13/pre-hygiene.md`.
  - Commit acf6cd7 (`docs(p13)`): this session log entry.
  - Commit this commit (`docs(p13/hygiene)`): corrective — the post-session `(Quorum|Zephyr)` verification grep flagged `charter/current-package.md`; the commit-4 close marker had carried the two former platform names as a use-mention. Close marker rephrased to a structural descriptor and this entry updated.

- Decisions: No new D-entries. Per hygiene-shape discipline — the three formalised shapes are already code-committed, and the find-replace plus accumulator-surface work is documentation hygiene.

- Tests: None. Documentation-only changes. No edits under `contexts/`, `apps/`, `shared_kernel/`, `padhanam/`, `tests/`, `infra/`, `migrations/`, `ops/`.

- Pre-write reconciliation: Five surfaces verified before commit 1. Surfaces 1, 4, 5 (schema.md structure, phase-2-audit-inputs.md state, current-package.md close marker) were mechanical — all three files in the assumed state, new content appends. Surface 2 (code substrates) was structural-honesty: four points of drift between the P12 audit-finding snapshots and current code, all absorbed into the schema.md content per AC 2 — `TenantContext.tenant_id` and `ErrorResponse.correlation_id` are typed `str` (UUID-shaped) not `UUID`; `TenantContext.__post_init__` validates all three fields, not two; `ErrorResponse` is a flat `BaseModel`, not a Pydantic discriminated union; the cursor-codec sites split three-singular `cursor.py` / one-plural `cursors.py` (optimization alone), not two/two, and the audit codec originates at S36, not S37. Surface 3 (grep) returned non-zero — four non-allowlist files — so commit 2 was a substantive find-replace, not the no-op the brief flagged as possible; the planned session held at five commits; a sixth corrective commit followed (see Produced). Three operator questions (status-snapshot exemption, Zephyr grep-widening, use-mention rephrasing) were resolved in prose ahead of commit 1.

- Reflection prompts answered:

  1. *Hygiene-session-as-shape, fourth instance.* The shape vocabulary holds cleanly. Commit counts across the four instances — pre-P12 hygiene (six), post-P12 charter-discipline hygiene (seven), pre-Phase-2 synthesis-and-refinement (ten), pre-P13 (five planned, six with a post-verification corrective) — track focus: carryover-closure is a tighter unit of work than structural-finding capture, discipline-convention establishment, or synthesis-and-refinement, and the five-commit planned shape is the smallest of the four. The third-instance temporal-position taxonomy (pre-audit, post-audit, pre-next-phase hygiene) does not absorb this instance: all three of those positions sit at phase boundaries, while this hygiene session sits intra-package — after P13 framing closed but before S43, the package's first build session, opens. It warrants a fourth sub-position, pre-package-first-session hygiene. A distinguishing structural property reinforces the new sub-position: this is the first hygiene session whose work surface was set by an explicit framing decision (P13 framing Decision 2 bundled the two items) rather than by carryover triaged at hygiene-open time. First-instance evidence for the pre-package-first-session sub-shape; recurrence test fires at the next package framing that bundles hygiene into a pre-first-session session.

  2. *Pre-write reconciliation, twelfth-plus instance.* The five surfaces split three mechanical (schema.md structure, phase-2-audit-inputs.md state, current-package.md close marker — each file in its assumed state) and one structural-honesty (surface 2's code-substrate verification) plus one that drove commit-shape (surface 3's grep). Surface 2 surfaced four points of drift between the P12 audit-finding snapshots and current code state — every field-table type and structural claim in the brief's schema.md content needed at least one correction, and the cursor-codec sites table had both a wrong path and a wrong originating session. The discipline earned its place decisively here: surface 2 alone caught four documentation errors that would otherwise have shipped into a binding-specification surface and propagated forward as ground truth. Surface 3's grep returned non-zero (four non-allowlist files), so commit 2 stayed substantive rather than compressing to the brief's contingency no-op. The twelfth-plus instance confirms the pattern is past any promotion threshold; the open question is no longer whether to keep it but whether to push verification one step upstream (the framing-landing session's methodology line already proposed a brief-authoring-time D-number checkpoint; the same logic applies to code-substrate snapshots — a brief that cites code field-shapes should verify them against current code at brief-authoring time).

  3. *Grep allowlist as cross-context discipline.* The S13 verification-grep allowlist began as a rebrand-sub-shape playbook element — a literal three-path list (`charter/decisions.md`, `log/sessions.md`, `docs/archive/`). This session generalises it on three axes, each a first-instance observation with a recurrence test at the next narrative-cleanup operation. (a) The allowlist is not a closed enumeration but an expression of an append-only-protected *category* defined by a structural property: any file that records what was true at a specific past moment, regardless of directory. Dated point-in-time snapshots (`docs/notes/status-*.md`) qualify by construction; so do verbatim-by-mandate build-session briefs under `briefs/` — surfaced at commit 4 when preserving this very prompt, whose title carries the old platform name. Rephrasing such a file would falsify its record, a worse violation of append-only spirit than the literal grep-rule violation. (b) The rebrand-cleanup discipline operates at the principle level — old-platform-name scrubbing from current-facing narrative — not the literal-string level; this is why the verification grep widened from `Quorum` to `(Quorum|Zephyr)`, since Zephyr is structurally equivalent (an old platform name, archived by D38, legitimate in historical content only). One operation, not two. (c) Use-mention occurrences require structural-descriptor rephrasing, not direct find-replace: `p13-epic.md` named this hygiene task itself, where a literal replace produces nonsense and allowlist-extension creates a permanent stale-name carrier. The S13 rebrand playbook did not anticipate use-mention because S13 was the rebrand event, not a post-rebrand cleanup; this session is the first post-rebrand cleanup operation.

  4. *Phase 2-A close hygiene list as new convention surface.* Commit 3 established the "Phase 2-A close hygiene list" section in `charter/phase-2-audit-inputs.md` as the accumulator for closure-housekeeping items triaged to the Phase 2-A close per P13 framing Decision 2. It is structurally distinct from two adjacent surfaces: the substrate-completion list and `charter/deferred-decisions.md` both carry activation-gated deferred work (substrate awaiting explicit trigger conditions), whereas this list carries closure-housekeeping with no activation gate — items clear at the Phase 2-A close hygiene session regardless of trigger. The roadmap stale-headers refresh lands as the first entry. First-instance evidence for the convention; recurrence test fires at the first item added through Phase 2-A package execution beyond this opening entry. One placement observation: the section co-locates with four audit-input sections in a file oriented to the Phase 2 close audit; close-hygiene and audit-input are different categories, and the co-location holds per the brief's explicit instruction (both are Phase 2 forward-accumulators) but is the kind of surface a future hygiene session might split if the list grows.

- methodology (line 1): **Pre-package-first-session hygiene is a fourth temporal sub-position for the hygiene-session shape.** The prior three-position taxonomy (pre-audit, post-audit, pre-next-phase) is phase-boundary-scoped; this instance sits intra-package between package framing close and the first build session, with its work surface set by an explicit framing decision rather than accumulated carryover. First-instance evidence; see reflection prompt 1.

- methodology (line 2): **The narrative-cleanup verification-grep allowlist is an append-only-protected category defined by structural property, not a literal path list.** Any file that records what was true at a past moment — `docs/archive/`, dated `docs/notes/status-*.md` snapshots, verbatim-preserved `briefs/` prompts — qualifies; rephrasing one would falsify its record. First-instance generalisation; see reflection prompt 3(a).

- methodology (line 3): **Rebrand-cleanup operates at the principle level — old-platform-name scrubbing — not the literal-string level.** The verification grep widened to every former platform name (`Quorum` and `Zephyr`) because the workitem's principle is drift removal, not a single search term. First-instance evidence; see reflection prompt 3(b).

- methodology (line 4): **Use-mention occurrences in rebrand-cleanup require structural-descriptor rephrasing, not direct find-replace.** Where text names the cleanup task or the rename event rather than referring to the platform by its old name, a literal replace produces nonsense; the generic descriptor preserves clarity without carrying the literal. First post-rebrand-cleanup instance; see reflection prompt 3(c).

- methodology (line 5): **The post-session verification grep caught drift the session introduced in its own close marker.** Commit 4's close marker named the two former platform names as a use-mention; the post-commit-5 `(Quorum|Zephyr)` grep flagged `charter/current-package.md`, corrected at commit 6. The verification step is not redundant with careful authoring — it caught an own-goal authoring missed. A narrative-cleanup session should run its verification grep after every commit, including the commits that describe the cleanup.

- **Pre-P13 hygiene closed; S43 next per `briefs/p13/s43.md`.**

- Pre-write reconciliation: Five surfaces verified before any commit landed; three findings surfaced and were operator-dispositioned in prose ahead of execution. Finding A: the three architecture-primitive D-numbers were rotated in the drafted artefacts (artefacts said D116 tiered-by-salience / D117 two-vector decay / D118 three-tier consent; `charter/decisions.md` and `charter/architecture.md` bind D116 three-tier consent / D117 tiered-by-salience / D118 two-vector decay). Finding B: the drafted roadmap v6 used a `## v6` heading with labelled paragraphs; the existing "## Version log" convention is single-paragraph bullets — v6 reformatted to a `- **v6**` bullet, all content preserved. Finding C: the deferred-decisions entries were not supplied verbatim; the eight entries were authored from the Artefact 1 classification table into a new `## Phase 2-A P13 framing deferrals` section with a TOC entry per the TOC-maintenance discipline. - Reflection prompts answered:

  1. *Pre-conversation decisions audit.* The brief carried seven pre-conversation recommendations; the substantive conversation revised or refined five and adopted two as written (per close deliverable 1). The brief's named token source `brand-tokens.css` is not linked into `deck.html` and is a separate dark-mode application design system; the deck is self-contained with its own inline `:root`. `log/sessions.md` is newest-at-top, not append-at-bottom as the brief stated. The brief referenced a comparison table "per the strategic conversation that produced this prompt" but did not transcribe it, and it was not preserved anywhere in the repo. A nine-column table with paragraph-length cells would not render cleanly in the deck's 720px prose column. The deferred-decisions insertion point and the current-package close-marker placement both resolved cleanly. The missing-table-content finding is what triggered the scope revision.

  2. *Methodology comparison as Phase 1 close substrate.* The comparison did not land as a deck act, so it creates no immediate reading-pressure on the Phase 1 close audit. The maintenance-discipline entry still creates that pressure independently: it commits the methodology document's first prescriptive articulation of continuous-operation discipline to the Phase 1 close authoring session. The multi-implementer hypothesis sits correctly as a deferred-decisions entry with a much-later activation horizon and consumes no methodology-document oxygen at Phase 1 close.

  3. *Differential cell treatment as discipline.* The Padhanam row's four postures — truly-TBD on reverse engineering, phase-deferred on throughput, forward commitment on maintenance, forward hypothesis on multi-implementer — are preserved in the `methodology-comparison.md` draft. The honest-accounting discipline held even as the artefact itself was deferred: the draft is explicitly marked unverified, the third-party claims carry accuracy flags, and the deferral is logged rather than the artefact rushed to publication.

  4. *Methodology-document promotion implications.* No promotion candidate at this session. The brief's proposed candidate (comparative-artefact discipline plus posture-differentiation) is first-instance and does not meet the captures-candidate threshold of structural novelty or threefold recurrence. The session does surface one genuinely novel observation, recorded as a methodology line below.

- methodology (line 1): **Pre-write reconciliation can surface a readiness gap in the deliverable itself, not only drift in facts.** The brief specified a deck act and referenced a comparison table it did not contain. Pre-write reconciliation surfaced the absence; the disciplined response was to re-scope the deliverable (draft now, verify and publish later) rather than fabricate content to fill the gap. First instance of reconciliation catching a deliverable-readiness gap as distinct from a fact-drift gap; recurrence test at future briefs that reference un-transcribed substrate.

- methodology (line 2): **First load-bearing application of the proper-noun-attribution discipline.** The "Methodology mechanical-enforcement upgrades" deferred entry names a proper-noun-attribution check on model-drafted vendor-voice artefacts. This session is the first time that discipline blocked a publication: the comparison table's claims about six named third-party methodologies were judged not verification-ready for a public artefact, and the operator deferred publication. The discipline anticipated exactly this class of content. Worth noting at the Phase 1 close audit as evidence the deferred-decisions backlog item is correctly specified.

A separate content-payload gap surfaced at session open and was load-bearing: the commit-session prompt was a template with eight content placeholders ("[problem paragraph verbatim from the Step 1 conversation close]" and seven similar) rather than a content-bearing prompt; the Step 1 deliverables and the Step 1 conversation prompt verbatim were not in the commit-session prompt as paste-ready substitutions. The operator restored both via a follow-up message before commit 1 could execute. This is methodology line 2 below.

- Reflection prompt 2: **Step 1 close as Phase 2 design entry.** Charter-grade placement per Decision 1 of the Step 1 conversation makes the 7-Step arc binding specification for Phase 2's strategic shape. Step 3's prioritised bets become the Phase 2 LVT structure landing as v6 roadmap entry per D44; the arc's full close produces the bets that Phase 2 packaging organises around. The commitment pressure on subsequent Steps is real: each Step now operates under the discipline of producing charter-grade content that survives audit, rather than producing notes-grade content that can be discarded if it does not converge. Subsequent Steps may surface friction with this pressure — for example, if Step 4 (synthesis of root causes) does not converge cleanly within a single strategic-mode session, the arc holds the open questions explicitly rather than defer to "later phases." The charter-grade placement is the structural commitment that the arc converges to landing; deferral to a later phase becomes a charter-superseding act rather than a quiet defer. Forward-relevance: the placement also makes the arc auditable at the next phase audit (Phase 2 close); a procurement reader can trace the arc's outputs through the LVT structure to the substrate Phase 2 builds, with each Step's deliverables sitting in the same charter file as the binding specification chain.

- Reflection prompt 3: **Methodology dogfooding-evidence record as a Phase 1 deliverable.** The bet's methodology-embedding claim now has its first structural-level evidence in committed charter form. The dogfooding-evidence record names specifically what the McKinsey 7-Step ProblemFramer role authored at S26b informed (function-focused system_prompt with scope/situation/complication/success-criteria shape; SCQ override layering as light additional discipline; the "you frame, not analyse" framing-versus-analysis distinction) and where the template's scope did not cover the conversation's expanded discipline (four of the five conversation sub-prompts — Who has the problem, What gets worse if the problem persists, How would success be measured, What is the problem NOT — extend beyond what the role's system_prompt encodes; only "What is the problem" maps directly). The record's honest framing is what makes it procurement-grade rather than performative: it names where the template earned its place and where it did not, and it logs the methodology-extension workitem (expand the role's system_prompt to encode the five-sub-prompt discipline, or land via the recommended-skills-per-role surface deferred to Phase 2 per `briefs/p8/mckinsey-7-step.md`). Phase 2 packaging benefits from the workitem being concrete and structurally located: it absorbs either as part of the methodology-as-product UX surface or as a separate methodology-extension session early in Phase 2. The case for landing it as an early Phase 2 package strengthens because structural-level evidence is the minimum bar, behavioural dogfooding (an actual ProblemFramer agent producing the same problem statement through the runtime) is the higher bar, and the methodology-extension workitem is on the path to that higher bar.

- Reflection prompt 4: **Blank-sheet discipline scope verdict.** Decision 2 of the Step 1 conversation chose option (a): blank-sheet within the bet's success criteria. Step 1's deliverables surface drift relative to two Phase 2-specific commitments. First, D93's methodology-as-product framing positions the audience as senior product leaders and consultancies; the Step 1 problem statement names "busy professionals working twelve-plus hour days" with a portfolio integrating work and personal goals, which is broader. The drift is not catastrophic — methodology-as-product can apply to either audience — but it signals that D93's audience framing may need supersession or refinement at Step 3 when prioritised bets land. Second, the four-functions framework at `charter/product-methodology.md` (PM, Marketing, L&D, PgM) covers professional functions only; the Step 1 problem statement implies personal-life portfolio integration that the four functions do not cover. Either a fifth function or a framework expansion will be needed; this is the deferred-to-Phase-2-strategic-opening workitem named in the prompt's Out of scope. The blank-sheet discipline scope held: the bet's core claims remain intact; the Phase 2-specific commitments are the layer surfacing drift, which is exactly where Decision 2 placed the scope. Step 2 (Disaggregation) or Step 3 (Prioritisation) likely absorbs the refinement work; expanding scope to question the bet itself at this point would reframe the entire arc and is not warranted by the Step 1 evidence. The scope choice was sound.

- methodology (line 1): **Briefs/ preservation convention does not survive without explicit operator restoration across strategic-block sequences.** Three blocks since P9 open (pre-Phase-2 architecture synthesis, post-P12 charter-discipline hygiene, P12 audit) did not preserve their briefs at `briefs/`. The operator's restoration at this Step 1 commit re-establishes the convention with the `briefs/phase-2/` directory and slug-based naming. The lapse-and-restoration shape suggests the convention needs structural support beyond memory: either a methodology-document explicit prescription for strategic-mode blocks (the existing Session brief preservation sub-section names the `briefs/<package>/<session>.md` format but does not enforce it across all session types and does not name the strategic-block slug pattern explicitly), or a mechanical-enforcement upgrade. Patterns-observed candidate: "Briefs/ preservation convention lapse pattern at strategic-mode block sequences"; first explicit instance logged here.

- methodology (line 2): **Placeholder-versus-content-payload miss in commit-session prompts authored against Claude.ai-conversation deliverables.** This commit-session prompt was structured as a template with eight content placeholders for content the operator provided in a follow-up message. The placeholder shape was honest (the prompt explicitly named what each placeholder slot expected) but the content was not pasted into the prompt before commit execution. Two viable conventions for future commit-session prompts: (a) author the prompt after the Claude.ai conversation closes, pasting deliverables inline so the prompt is content-bearing at the moment Claude Code starts; (b) author the prompt as a template with explicit pre-execution instruction ("commit-session prompt is a template; before Claude Code begins, paste content into placeholders"). The first is the structurally honest shape because the commit-session prompt is itself preservation-bearing per D43; preserving a template-with-placeholders is preserving incomplete content. The second has a robustness advantage when commit-session prompt authoring lags Claude.ai conversation close. Recurrence test continues at the next Claude.ai-conversation-to-Claude-Code commit-session transition (likely Step 2's commit session).

- **Phase 2 design 7-Step arc Step 1 commit closed.** The next strategic-mode block is Phase 2 design 7-Step arc Step 2 (Disaggregation), opening at Claude.ai. Step 2 takes the seven initial-disaggregation candidates and produces a MECE-decomposed issue tree per the McKinsey 7-Step Disaggregator role's discipline, addresses the Step 2 open question on substrate-integration cut, and lands its artefacts as a Step 2 section in `charter/phase-2-design-7step.md` via a subsequent commit session. Step 3 prioritises and produces the bets that land as Phase 2 LVT structure at the v6 roadmap entry per D44 plus the Phase 2 PRD per D43.

- Findings outcomes per the brief's pre-write reconciliation framing:

  - **Finding 2 (methodology refinement scope coupling).** Three refinements landed without surfacing deeper restructuring concerns. Foundation super-section creation absorbed the existing two H2 sections (What Padhanam is, What's being investigated) plus the new Hypothesis and iteration sub-section cleanly under one H2. Work organisation super-section restructure consolidated four H2s (Work hierarchy, Frameworks, Session shape, Session shapes) into six H3 sub-sections under one H2; existing content preserved verbatim within new sub-section structure; new Phase lifecycle, Package lifecycle, and Session lifecycle sub-sections drafted from scratch with substantive prose. Measurement model hypothesis-framing single-paragraph insertion plus cross-reference at "What the numbers are not". No operator engagement required; the disposition rule applied autonomously.

Diagrams use `flowchart TB` and `flowchart LR` shapes with subgraph clustering where load-bearing (the four-context substrate diagram has four subgraphs grouping producers, consumer, rules, transport).

Architecture.md does not include explicit `docs/archive/decisions/phase-1.md` path links per D-entry to keep the prose readable; the principles.md three-document pointer establishes the navigation convention at the entry surface and readers follow the D-entry references with the archive-path as canonical destination.

- Reflection prompt 1: **Architecture.md as procurement-grade synthesis verdict.** The seven-section structure held up under content authoring without surfacing structural refinements during writing. The five Mermaid diagrams carry the load-bearing structural information that prose alone would require multiple paragraphs each to convey: the twelve-bounded-context structure plus padhanam/ plus shared_kernel/ surfaces as a single diagram in Architectural patterns; the database-per-tenant topology with TenantContext propagation arrows surfaces in Tenancy and jurisdiction; the vendor port-adapter pattern with concrete adapters per vendor surfaces in Vendor and dependency posture; the four-layer constraint stack ordering (platform invariants as floor, methodology opt-in, workflow opt-in, agent layer per-instance) surfaces in Domain primitives; the four-context evaluation-to-recommendation pipeline with HTTP transport surfaces the bet's criterion-4 demonstration in The four-context substrate. The Cross-document map section is the structural anchor that makes architecture.md sit cleanly in the charter rather than overlapping unhelpfully with principles.md or decisions.md; the three-document relationship is clean (principles for binding rules; decisions for full reasoning; architecture for synthesis with diagrams) and the principles header pointer added at commit 9 plus the methodology Charter structure update establish the relationship across surfaces. Forward-relevance: Phase 2 close audit reviews architecture.md against the as-built Phase 2 substrate; v2 will accrete the workflow context substrate per D83, the four producer→consumer-UX-layer pipeline if Phase 2 surfaces a fifth context, and any Phase 2-period architectural commitments that don't fit current sections cleanly. Sections likely to need v2 refinement: The four-context substrate (Phase 2 workflow + UX layer additions); Domain primitives (Phase 2 may add UX-layer domain concepts). Sections likely to remain stable through Phase 2: Architectural patterns (the hexagonal-and-bounded-contexts shape is bet-grade architecture); Tenancy and jurisdiction (the database-per-tenant + jurisdiction commitments are bet-grade); Vendor and dependency posture (the vendor-flexibility commitment is bet-grade per D111).

- Reflection prompt 2: **Methodology refinements coherence verdict.** The three refinements integrated cleanly with v2 super-section grouping. Foundation super-section landed as a natural grouping for content that was already conceptually grouped but not structurally; the new Hypothesis and iteration sub-section reads as a natural extension of "What's being investigated" rather than as a substantial reframing — the iteration cycle was implicit throughout the v2 document and v3 makes it explicit. Work organisation super-section restructure was the most substantive change; the six-sub-section structure (Strategic tree, Phase lifecycle, Package lifecycle, Session lifecycle, Session shapes, Frameworks) makes lifecycle disciplines visible without bloating the document because the new sub-sections (Phase lifecycle, Package lifecycle, Session lifecycle) replace prose that was previously distributed across multiple H2s. Measurement model hypothesis-framing is a one-paragraph addition that operates as a meta-commitment about the model itself; the framing was implicit at v2 and v3 makes it explicit. The methodology document and architecture.md are properly delimited: methodology covers how Padhanam itself is built (operator discipline, build process, framing-prompt patterns, refactoring conventions, session shapes, measurement model); architecture covers what Padhanam is built as (architectural commitments, patterns, contexts, vendor posture, domain primitives, substrate scaffolding). No overlap; the Cross-document map in architecture.md and the Charter structure sub-section in methodology.md establish the boundary explicitly. Future hygiene at Phase 2 audit may surface additional refinement candidates if Phase 2 evidence demands; the v3 hypothesis is that Foundation + Work organisation + Measurement model refinements absorb the post-P12 evidence and the document is sustainable through Phase 2 without further structural change.

- Reflection prompt 3: **Three-document relationship operationalisation.** The three-document relationship holds up structurally at this session's close. The principles.md header pointer (architecture.md for synthesis; decisions.md for full reasoning; methodology.md for build discipline) establishes the navigation convention at the entry surface readers reach first. The methodology document's Charter structure sub-section names architecture.md as a fourth Strategic constant alongside bet.md, principles.md, methodology.md; the strategic-constants framing makes the four-document relationship visible. The architecture.md Cross-document map section names principles + decisions + architecture as the three reading-mode triad (session-open compact; cold-path audit-time; warm-path synthesis-time) and names methodology + product-methodology as adjacent surfaces (build methodology; product methodology layer for users). Together the three cross-references describe a coherent reading order: at session open, read principles.md (compact rules); for full reasoning, read decisions.md (active phase) or the per-phase archive; for synthesis, read architecture.md (with diagrams); for build discipline, read methodology.md; for product methodology, read product-methodology.md. Each document has a distinct audience and a distinct reading moment. Phase 2 framing benefits from reading architecture.md alongside the bet, the PRFAQ, principles.md, decisions.md (active), and the consolidated Phase 2 inputs file: architecture.md gives the procurement-grade-touch synthesis surface that Phase 2 framing's strategic-mode block reads as the structural anchor for "what we have built and how it relates to the proposition". Forward-relevance for Phase 2 close: architecture.md v2 absorbs Phase 2 substrate additions; methodology.md v4 absorbs Phase 2 discipline-pattern evidence; principles.md gains new principles as Phase 2 surfaces commitments worth promoting to binding rules; decisions.md (Phase 2 active) accumulates Phase 2 D-entries until Phase 2 close, at which point Phase 2 D-entries archive to `docs/archive/decisions/phase-2.md` per the per-phase archival pattern.

- methodology (line 1): **Pre-Phase-2 strategic-mode session is a third hygiene-shape instance.** First instance was pre-P12 hygiene at P11 close (six commits, structural-finding focus). Second instance was post-P12 charter-discipline hygiene at the post-audit boundary (seven commits, discipline-convention focus). Third instance is this session (ten commits, synthesis-and-refinement focus). The accumulation of three hygiene-shape sessions at three different temporal positions (mid-P11-close, post-audit, pre-Phase-2-framing) reinforces the methodology document v2's framing that hygiene sessions plan by default at phase boundaries. The three instances also surface a sub-shape distinction worth naming for future audit observation: pre-audit hygiene (P11 close) addresses substrate-session debt; post-audit hygiene (post-P12) addresses charter-discipline debt the audit surfaced but explicitly scoped out; pre-next-phase hygiene (this session) addresses synthesis-and-cleaned-state-for-framing needs that the audit-output state doesn't fully satisfy. The three sub-shapes may consolidate or stay distinct at Phase 2 close audit observation.

- methodology (line 2): **Architecture.md as new charter surface absorbs synthesis-density load that principles.md cannot carry without losing scannability and decisions.md cannot carry without losing per-D-entry independence.** The three-document relationship resolves a tension implicit at Phase 1 that surfaced explicitly at P12 close: principles.md is read every session and must stay tight; decisions.md carries 113 D-entries each with Choice/Reasoning/Alternatives/Kano and is structurally optimised for per-entry audit-trail not for cross-entry synthesis. Procurement-grade-touch reading requires synthesis with diagrams that neither surface naturally accommodates. Architecture.md as a third synthesis surface fills the gap. The Cross-document map in architecture.md plus the principles header pointer plus the methodology Charter structure update operationalise the relationship across all three surfaces; the convention scales to v2 of architecture.md at Phase 2 close. Phase 2 close audit reviews the relationship's coherence under Phase 2 evidence (does architecture.md still synthesise cleanly when Phase 2 adds UX layer concepts and workflow context substrate? does the principles header pointer remain accurate? does the methodology Charter structure list need expansion?).

- methodology (line 3): **Methodology document refinements absorbed structurally without v3 surfacing tensions.** The v3 hypothesis is that Foundation hypothesis-iteration, Work organisation lifecycle expansion, and Measurement model hypothesis-framing make the document's hypothesis-iteration nature visible at the structural level rather than only at the content level. v1 articulated the discipline; v2 absorbed Phase 1 evidence into Patterns-observed entries; v3 surfaces the iteration mechanic explicitly so that Phase 2's evidence has a structural place to land. The hypothesis-as-living-cycle framing in Hypothesis and iteration sub-section makes the methodology's revision posture explicit ("the methodology document is a living hypothesis revised across phase boundaries"); the Work organisation lifecycle structure makes phase-and-package-and-session boundaries visible at the document's level rather than only at the project's level; the Measurement model hypothesis-framing makes the metric model itself a hypothesis. Forward-relevance: Phase 2 close audit reviews whether v3's structural reframing actually absorbs Phase 2's evidence without further structural change, or whether v4 needs additional super-sections (e.g., a dedicated cross-cutting Patterns super-section grouping Patterns-observed plus Failure-modes-observed plus What-Padhanam-does-not-do plus the principle sub-sections at the document's tail).

- **Pre-Phase-2 architecture synthesis and methodology refinement closed.** The next strategic-mode block is Phase 2 packaging. No substantive compression landed; the substantive work was the three cross-reference additions (Mechanical enforcement upgrades sub-section under Architectural enforcement, phase-audit-template extension under Reflection and learning, refined Failure-modes cross-reference at the fabrication-class entry).

  - **Finding 2 (D-entry reference precision in principles audit).** Five principles required multi-D references reflecting combined commitments. The structurally honest pattern is to list both D-entries in the parenthetical (D4+D16 for hexagonal where D4 is no-vendor-SDKs and D16 is bounded-contexts-on-hexagonal; D1+D32 for database-per-tenant where D1 is the model and D32 is the per-tenant instance topology; D4+D15 for LiteLLM+Ollama where D4 is the gateway and D15 is the model default; D14+D76 for customer-specific behaviour where D14 is the original commitment and D76 is the forking-phrasing refinement; D16+D17+D28 for bounded contexts where D16 is the codebase structure, D17 is the cross-context communication shape, and D28 is the cross-cutting padhanam/ package). Single-D references at fourteen principles. The autonomous-disposition rule applied to all twenty-two reference additions without operator engagement because the load-bearing D-entry for each principle was unambiguous on inspection.

  - **Finding 3 (deferred-decisions thematic cluster placement edge cases).** Forty-three entries mapped cleanly to one of the eight clusters. Three placement edge cases the brief anticipated resolved autonomously: Per-tenant supply-chain surveillance for tenant-supplied tools placed at Compliance and security (the "surveillance" mechanism is the load-bearing concern, not the "tools" subject); Scheduled-runs primitive placed at Architectural primitives (the platform-vs-external-trigger choice is the load-bearing decision, not the consumer use case); Optimization-engine cost-per-successful-task threshold tuning placed at Phase 1 close audit findings (the entry was added during the P12 audit per audit-inputs entry 8 disposition, grouping it with other P12-audit-touched entries was the structurally honest cluster). No operator engagement on placement; the cluster-choice was clear at inspection.

- Pre-write reconciliation finding at commit 4: the brief named "HTTP API for evaluation management" alongside "HTTP API for ingestion management" for Status closure marking. Codebase inspection surfaced that no dedicated deferred-decisions entry exists for evaluation-management HTTP; the topic is named only as a short-form carryover in `charter/current-package.md`. D112 absorbed the work at S42. The structurally honest move is to mark only the ingestion-management entry (the entry that exists) rather than retroactively author an entry just to mark it closed. The finding mirrors the mid-build pre-write reconciliation sub-pattern from the methodology document v2 — brief-vs-codebase reconciliation surfacing at execution time, resolved with explicit disposition recorded in the commit message and this session log entry. Recurrence test continues: the methodology document v2's Patterns-observed entry on pre-write reconciliation absorbed this instance as the seventeenth-plus instance spanning S6 through this session.

- Reflection prompt 1: **Charter discipline conventions operationalisation.** Three new conventions landed at the methodology document as natural extensions of existing content. The per-phase decisions archival pattern reads as a clean parallel to D107's per-package session-log pattern (same shape at a different temporal granularity; the discipline is "archival cadence matches the natural review boundary"); the deferred-entry closure-by-D-entry convention reads as a natural specialisation of the existing append-only discipline (closed entries remain visible with explicit status rather than disappearing or editing); the principles-D-entry reference convention codifies a pattern already present at five principles before this session (D82, D86, D107, D111, D113) and generalises to the twenty-two newly-referenced principles at commit 3. None of the three required new sub-section structure beyond what the existing Charter structure / Append-only discipline sections accommodated; the conventions landed as sub-sub-sections within existing scope. Forward-relevance for Phase 2 inheritance: the conventions are structured well enough that Phase 2 sessions inherit them as discipline rather than as ceremonial pattern. The decisions-archival pattern in particular will fire at Phase 2 close when D-entries from Phase 2 archive to `docs/archive/decisions/phase-2.md`; the per-phase-summary-plus-archive-pointer format established at commit 6 carries forward as the canonical pattern. The conventions function as charter substrate the next phase audit reads against without re-derivation.

- Reflection prompt 2: **Methodology document maintenance pass observations.** Finding 1's "compression candidate" framing presupposed that some sections might fail the earning-place test. The audit found none. Three observations carry forward to the next methodology document maintenance moment. First, the document's earning-place posture is structurally durable because the document was authored from inception as the narrative companion to principles+decisions; the document doesn't repeat the "what" of D-entries (their Choice text is the canonical source) and doesn't repeat the "what" of principles (their concise statements are the canonical source); the document carries the "why" content (reasoning, recurrence counts, mechanism, examples) that the other two surfaces structurally exclude. Second, the document's size is sustainable through Phase 2 if the same earning-place posture holds; v2 grew by ~120 lines at the P12 audit through six new Patterns-observed entries plus the Session shapes sub-section, and v3 at Phase 2 close will grow by a similar magnitude through Phase 2's accumulated discipline-pattern observations. The compression pattern in the brief was anticipatory rather than necessary; future maintenance passes may surface real compression candidates if the document accretes elaboration that doesn't earn its place (e.g., a sub-section that restates a D-entry's reasoning rather than narrating the "why" beyond the D-entry). Third, the cross-reference refactor at commit 2 (Mechanical enforcement upgrades sub-section under Architectural enforcement) demonstrates a pattern worth naming: when the methodology document's discipline-articulation lives at a deferred-decisions entry, both surfaces benefit from bidirectional cross-references (the discipline articulation at the methodology document; the activation triggers at the deferred-decisions entry). The pattern generalises beyond mechanical-enforcement upgrades; any methodology discipline that has both a "what is the discipline" surface and a "when does it activate" surface fits the bidirectional-cross-reference pattern.

- Reflection prompt 3: **Decisions archival pattern observations.** First execution of the per-phase decisions archival pattern at Phase 1 close. The archival cleanly reduced the active `charter/decisions.md` from 1589 lines to 136 lines, well below the AC 4 target of <400 lines. The one-line-summary plus archive-pointer format reads well at session-open (every D-entry's title, package, session, and date are visible without scrolling more than a few screens; supersession relationships and revision relationships called out inline; archive pointer at the file header rather than per-line keeps the surface tight). The format proved expressive enough to call out supersession and revision relationships inline (D3 superseded by D52; D14's forking phrasing refined by D76; D28's package-name supersession by D38; D45's voice/audience supersession by D51 and D93; D88's sync signature revised by D90; D74's v2 at D81; D29's product-name supersession by D38; D39's pending-authorship framing superseded by D113). Three observations carry forward to Phase 2 close. First, the per-phase archival cadence is the right granularity for a project this size; per-session archival would be too fine, per-bet (every-N-phases) would be too coarse and would let the working file accrete unsustainably. Second, the summary format may evolve to be richer for entries with significant supersession or revision relationships (the inline "Superseded by ..." or "Revised at ..." annotations are useful but could become structured fields if Phase 2 introduces enough supersession to make the relationships hard to track); Phase 2 close audit reviews based on accumulated evidence. Third, the archival file at `docs/archive/decisions/phase-1.md` should not be edited after this commit per the append-only discipline; corrections to D-entry content from this point forward land via new D-entries that supersede the original, both in the archive (the original D-entry stands untouched) and as a new summary line in the active file pointing at the new D-entry's archive location. The active file's summary lines for archived D-entries are themselves append-only-at-the-summary-line level (the title and metadata may be corrected for accuracy but the entry is not removed or substantively edited; the canonical D-entry content lives at the archive).

- methodology (line 1): **Hygiene session at the phase boundary is the second instance of the hygiene-shaped session pattern.** The first instance was pre-P12 hygiene at P11 close (six commits, six items, structural-finding documentation). This second instance fires at the boundary between P12 audit close and Phase 2 packaging open (seven commits, ten items, charter-discipline focus). Recurrence rate: two instances at two phase boundaries (P11 close, post-P12-audit) within the same phase (Phase 1). The methodology document v2's Session shapes sub-section names hygiene as a recognized shape; the second instance reinforces the planning-by-default framing for Phase 2 (substrate-session work consistently produces hygiene-shaped end-of-package debt; planning hygiene sessions in advance is structurally honest). Distinguishing characteristic of the post-audit-hygiene sub-shape vs the end-of-package-hygiene sub-shape: post-audit-hygiene addresses charter-discipline debt surfaced *during* the audit but explicitly out-of-scope of the audit per the audit's no-code-changes framing; end-of-package-hygiene addresses substrate-session debt accumulated during the package's build sessions. Both qualify as hygiene-shaped sessions in the v2 methodology document's framing; the distinction is descriptive sub-classification rather than separate shape promotion.

- methodology (line 2): **Pre-write reconciliation fired once at commit 4 (HTTP API for evaluation management entry missing).** Mid-execution finding rather than session-open finding per the v2 methodology document's pre-write reconciliation sub-pattern framing. The brief named two Status-marker actions at commit 4 (ingestion-management closed by D104; evaluation-management closed by D112); codebase inspection at execution time surfaced that no dedicated entry exists for evaluation-management. Resolution: mark the one that exists, document the gap in the commit message, surface in the session log entry. The cost of the reconciliation moment was small (one paragraph in the commit message, two paragraphs here); the alternative of retroactively creating an entry just to mark it closed would have been ceremony against the append-only discipline. The methodology document's pre-write reconciliation pattern absorbed this instance as the seventeenth-plus instance; recurrence test continues at Phase 2 substrate sessions.

- methodology (line 3): **Charter-discipline conventions naturally fold into existing methodology document structure.** All three new conventions landed under existing sub-sections (Charter structure for two; Append-only discipline for one) rather than as standalone sub-sections. The structural pattern: discipline conventions that scale across multiple charter surfaces fit cleanly under the existing structural sub-sections that already organize those surfaces. Forward-relevance: future discipline conventions (Phase 2 may surface new ones during substrate or audit work) inherit the pattern; the methodology document's existing Charter structure / Append-only discipline / Architectural enforcement / Reflection and learning sub-sections accommodate convention-shaped content without sub-section proliferation. The methodology document scales by accreting content within existing sub-sections, not by adding new sub-sections at the top level; the top-level structure is the methodology's architectural shape and is stable across phases.

- **Post-P12 charter-discipline hygiene closed.** The next strategic-mode block is Phase 2 packaging. Phase 2 framing reads the cleaned charter surface (decisions.md at 136 lines, deferred-decisions.md reorganised into eight themed sub-sections with table of contents, methodology document v2 with the three new conventions and cross-references, principles.md with D-entry references throughout) plus the artefacts named at `charter/p12-phase-2-inputs.md` Phase 2 entry handoff section. Phase 2 framing produces a v6 roadmap entry per D44, a Phase 2 PRD per D43, and the first Phase 2 package's epic note per D43. The post-P12 hygiene work is complete; the charter surface is ready for Phase 2 framing to read against without re-derivation.

- Reflection prompt 1: **Phase 1 close verdict.** The bet's six load-bearing claims hold end-to-end at Phase 1 close. (1) A single tenant runs locally with the full stack: verified via `make up` plus per-tenant database routing per D32; the live-stack smoke at every P11 session reinforces. (2) One agent can be configured, run, audited, and optimised through the platform's own tooling: S30b demonstrated the configure-and-run surface for the LVT PM agent against tenant alpha producing a SMART problem statement; the McKinsey 7-Step ProblemFramer at tenant alpha producing a full Lean Value Tree closes the run surface; S37 demonstrated the audit surface; S41 demonstrated the optimise surface with rule-derived recommendations citing producer-context records with full evidence traceability. (3) The evaluation harness produces meaningful quality signals: S40 retrieval evaluation runner against the S40b clean gold-set produces recall@k differentials that distinguish retrieval-quality state across vector_only and graph_only strategies, with the all-zero CaveatAnnotation on graph_only flagging the infrastructure substrate state transparently per S40b's S41-evidence verdict. (4) The trace capture layer surfaces optimization recommendations, not just data: S41 produced run `3b538acb-...` with 2 retrieval_strategy recommendations citing S40b's c168c2ba and S39b's ef58678a both carrying CaveatAnnotation on graph_only's all-zero aggregates; cost_optimization substrate-honest zero emission against local dev costs; model_choice and prompt_revision skipped with structured substrate-gap reasons. A procurement reader can trace prose → rule → citation → producer-context records without ambiguity. The criterion's full close requires the Phase 2 UX deliverable per D93. (5) The operator can explain every architectural decision and why it was made: the decisions file at 112 D-entries each carrying Choice + Reasoning + Alternatives Considered + Kano is the demonstration artefact; the audit reads against this surface and verifies it against the as-built codebase. (6) The methodology document captures the architect-implementer pattern with enough specificity that another senior product leader could read it and adopt the discipline: the methodology document's substantive v2 update at this audit's commit 8 lifts the document from v1 articulation to v2 with Phase 1's accumulated discipline-pattern data points integrated; the document is the proprietary insight the platform demonstrates. Phase 1 closes with the substrate intact, the architectural discipline holding under measurement, and the methodology evidence ready for Phase 2 to consume as the substrate it builds against per D93's methodology-as-product positioning.

- Reflection prompt 2: **Audit-as-strategic-mode-session-shape methodology observation.** This session is the first phase audit at this project. The strategic-mode synthesis session shape held up cleanly as distinct from build sessions and from other strategic-mode work (framing decisions, charter authorship). Three observations carry forward to Phase 2 audit. First, the three-track structure (top-down D-entry verification, bottom-up codebase tour, audit-input disposition) provided complementary signal density at different surfaces; top-down surfaced one substantive drift finding (T2 D39 stale framing) plus eight non-action confirmations, bottom-up surfaced no code-level drift (the load-bearing verdict B7) plus six documentation-shaped observations, audit-input disposition consolidated fourteen P11-session candidates into seven methodology-document promotions plus four deferred-decisions actions plus three non-action confirmations. The three tracks did not duplicate effort; they did not conflict; their outputs composed into the cross-reference index cleanly. Second, the commit-shape granularity per the brief's eleven-commit split mapped cleanly onto the synthesis work — the commits grouped by destination file rather than chronologically per the brief, which produced git-log coherence at audit time. The skeleton-first-then-progressive-section-population pattern at commits 1 through 4 preserved the audit-trail value of seeing the findings document's evolution rather than landing it as a single all-content commit. Third, the no-code-changes-at-P12 framing held cleanly through the synthesis work; code-level corrections from audit findings (gold-set audit-emission back-fill, graph-extract reclaim policy) forward to follow-on sessions per the deferred-decisions refreshes, with the audit-findings document and the Phase 2 inputs file naming them with operational specificity for those sessions to inherit. Forward-relevance to Phase 2 audit: the audit-shape session works as a session shape; the methodology document v2 names it as the fifth session shape alongside substrate, bridge, transport, and hygiene; Phase 2 audit at the next phase boundary inherits the three-track structure plus the eleven-ish-commit-shape plus the no-code-changes framing plus the disposition vocabulary plus the audit-trail discipline (supersession via new D-entry rather than in-place edit, append-only correction sections on D-entries with build-time deviation, audit-findings document as the consolidated deliverable). The shape is reusable without rework at Phase 2 boundary.

- Reflection prompt 3: **Audit findings synthesis observations — signal density across the two surfaces.** Top-down D-entry verification produced higher signal density than bottom-up codebase tour, against my session-open expectation that they would produce equivalent volume. Top-down surfaced two substantive findings (T1 D109 audit-trail discipline; T2 D39 stale framing) plus seven non-action confirmations, totalling nine entries. Bottom-up surfaced seven entries but only one rose to substantive-finding altitude (B3 schema.md formalisation gap), with the other six landing as documentation-shape observations (twelve-context count B1, operator-utility endpoint B2, enforcement counts B4, test harness conventions B5, migration counts B6, no-drift verdict B7). The bottom-up surface's lower substantive density reflects the codebase's actually-clean-state: no work shipped without charter authorisation; the principles file's commitments hold against the codebase; the methodology's "architectural discipline survives AI-assisted implementation" load-bearing claim verified at structural level. The categories anticipated in pre-write reconciliation (commitment-versus-execution differences, partial implementation, silent supersession, abandoned alternatives, schema drift) held up at top-down but each fired at lower frequency than expected — silent supersession surfaced once (D14 wording at D76; explicit supersession at the time it was made), partial implementation surfaced zero times, abandoned alternatives surfaced zero times in the audit-relevant scope, schema drift surfaced once (T2 D39 framing; the only stale-framing finding). The category surprise the audit surfaced was the D39-pending-authorship-framing-as-charter-binding-residue: doc-content rebrand at pre-P12 hygiene did not propagate to charter binding-specification surfaces, which surfaced as five-plus drift sites at audit time. The lesson generalises beyond methodology document: doc-content edits at hygiene moments need explicit propagation-or-quarantine framing for charter binding-specification surfaces. The methodology document v2 absorbs the lesson into the "session shapes" sub-section's hygiene-session paragraph naming structural-finding documentation as part of the shape's deliverable surface. Forward-relevance to future audits: the top-down-versus-bottom-up signal asymmetry holds when the codebase tracks the charter cleanly; the asymmetry would invert if the codebase ships ahead of charter (the inverse drift pattern), which is a different methodology concern than what Phase 1 produces. Phase 2 audit at the next phase boundary reads against this pattern and assesses whether Phase 2's substrate-completion-versus-consumer-UX shape produces a different signal-asymmetry profile.

- methodology (line 1): **Audit-shape session is the fifth session shape per the methodology document v2 update.** The session log entry's existence under audit-mode declaration is itself the first instance of the audit shape; the methodology document's session shapes sub-section names the shape's distinguishing characteristics (three-track structure, no-code-changes framing, commit-grouping-by-destination-file rather than chronological, the disposition vocabulary, the audit-trail discipline). Phase 2 audit inherits the shape vocabulary at next-phase-boundary entry.

- methodology (line 2): **Methodology-candidate accumulation and audit-resolution rhythm fired as designed.** P11's six sessions accumulated fourteen methodology candidates consolidated at `charter/p12-audit-inputs.md` by the pre-P12 hygiene session; the P12 audit's commit-4 disposition mapped each candidate to one of four shapes (seven methodology-document promotions, three deferred-decisions refreshes, one deferred-decisions hold, three explicit non-action, plus three audit-surfaced entries beyond the original fourteen). The pattern's first explicit instance verified that the accumulation-then-audit rhythm distributes candidate-recognition work across the package while compressing synthesis into a single strategic-mode block at the phase boundary; methodology document v2 absorbs this as a Patterns-observed entry. Phase 2 work inherits the rhythm.

- methodology (line 3): **D113 supersession-without-rewrite discipline reinforces the existing append-only commitment.** D39 stands; D113 records the supersession explicitly; the principles.md amendment at commit 6 brings the binding-specification surface into accuracy without editing D39 in place. The pattern mirrors D52 (Phase 2 deferral of identity foundation superseding D3) and D76 (forking-principle phrasing refinement superseding D14's prior "forbidden" wording). The audit-trail value compounds at each instance: future readers see what was true at each point in time, including what was authored as "pending" before authorship completed; the methodology's evidentiary surface is the supersession trail itself, not just the latest state.

- **P11 plus P12 close Phase 1.** The next strategic-mode block is Phase 2 packaging. The packaging conversation reads the six artefacts named at `charter/p12-phase-2-inputs.md` Phase 2 entry handoff section (audit-findings document, audit-inputs file, methodology document v2, decisions.md including D113, deferred-decisions.md including the three refreshes, this inputs file) plus the bet, the PRFAQ at the candidate Phase 2-direction articulation, the principles file, and the roadmap at v5. ## Pre-P12 hygiene — P11 close consolidation and structural-finding capture
roles: analyst, technical writer, engineer
mode: build (P11 hygiene session; closes P11 session-level work before P12 phase audit)

- Produced: Six commits closed the pre-P12 hygiene session.
